# Backlog and Status Registry

This file is the canonical, tracked source of truth for project backlog and implementation status.

Do not use local-only files (for example `LOCAL_BACKLOG.md`) as canonical status references in committed docs.

## Canonical status vocabulary

- `active`: planned and in-scope
- `deferred`: intentionally postponed
- `blocked`: cannot progress until blocker is resolved
- `done_pending_verification`: implemented, but verification is incomplete
- `verified_done`: implemented and verified against code/tests
- `obsolete`: no longer relevant to current direction
- `superseded`: replaced by another item

## Priority vocabulary

- `p0`: urgent correctness/security impact
- `p1`: high product impact
- `p2`: normal planned work
- `p3`: low-priority improvement

## Evidence-first reconciliation workflow

For each status update:

1. Capture the claim source (doc path and text).
2. Verify in code and tests.
3. Set canonical status from evidence.
4. If conflicts exist, mark older claim as superseded and link back here.

## Comparative authority chain (explicit)

For comparative-program governance, authority order is:

1. **Canonical status authority (only):** this file (`docs/BACKLOG_STATUS.md`)
2. **Umbrella comparative strategy:** `docs/COMPREHENSIVE_COMPARATIVE_INTEGRATION_MASTER.md`
3. **Tool-specific deep-dive dossiers:** ABA, discovery-iso, clusterfile revised dossier, autoshiftv2 dossier
4. **Historical/background comparative docs:** non-primary unless explicitly promoted in `docs/INDEX.md`

Status conflicts are always resolved here, not in umbrella or per-tool comparative docs.

| DOC-074 | IPv6-only single-stack support (refactor enableIpv6 to separate IPv4/IPv6 toggles) | verified_done | p1 | User request (2026-05-17), OpenShift 4.20 IPv6-only support (available since 4.12+) | Commit 70a4433, tag v1.3.0, `docs/IPV6_PLATFORM_SUPPORT_MATRIX.md`, backend/src/index.js (schema v3), backend/src/generate.js (IPv6-only YAML generation), frontend/src/steps/NetworkingV2Step.jsx (IP stack mode selector), frontend/src/validation.js (mode-specific validation), backend/test/nic-bond-vlan-ipv6.test.js (IPv6-only test), all 232 tests passing | **Implementation complete (2026-05-18):** (1) **State schema v3:** Replaced hostInventory.enableIpv6 boolean with ipStackMode enum ('ipv4', 'ipv6', 'dual-stack'). Automatic migration: enableIpv6=true → ipStackMode='dual-stack', enableIpv6=false → ipStackMode='ipv4'. Backward compatibility: auto-detect dual-stack from IPv6 network presence. (2) **Platform support verified:** Bare Metal (Agent/IPI/UPI) ✅ all methods supported, vSphere (Agent/IPI/UPI) ✅ all methods supported, AWS GovCloud ❌ IPv4-only enforced, IBM Cloud IPI ❌ IPv4-only enforced. Documented in docs/IPV6_PLATFORM_SUPPORT_MATRIX.md. (3) **Frontend UI:** NetworkingV2Step IP stack mode selector (dropdown: IPv4 only/IPv6 only/Dual-stack). (4) **Backend generation:** buildInstallConfig/buildAgentConfig handle IPv6-only mode (single IPv6 network entries, no IPv4). buildClusterNetwork/buildServiceNetwork mode-specific CIDR generation. VIP handling: IPv6-only uses apiVipV6/ingressVipV6 only. nmstate generation: IPv4 disabled, IPv6 enabled for IPv6-only nodes. Platform-specific VIP emission (bare metal, vSphere, Nutanix). (5) **Validation:** validateIpStackModeRequirements function enforces mode-specific network requirements. IPv4-only: requires IPv4 machine network, cluster/service CIDRs, VIPs. IPv6-only: requires IPv6 machine network, cluster/service CIDRs, VIPs. Dual-stack: requires both. Per-node validation: static mode requires address matching IP stack mode. (6) **Testing:** Updated test fixtures (withDualStack uses ipStackMode, withBondNode/withVlanNode). Fixed bond/VLAN test node structures. Added IPv6-only cluster generation test (3-node HA cluster, verifies install-config has single IPv6 network entries, apiVIPs array with 1 IPv6 VIP, nmstate with IPv4 disabled/IPv6 enabled). All 232 tests passing. (7) **Files modified:** backend/src/index.js (schema v3 migration), backend/src/generate.js (IPv6-only logic), backend/src/fieldGuide/context.js (added ipStackMode), backend/test/fixtures/builders.js (updated helpers), backend/test/fixtures/base-states.js (ipStackMode defaults), backend/test/nic-bond-vlan-ipv6.test.js (IPv6-only test), frontend/src/steps/NetworkingV2Step.jsx (mode selector, conditional fields), frontend/src/steps/GlobalStrategyStep.jsx, frontend/src/steps/HostInventoryV2Step.jsx, frontend/src/steps/HostInventoryStep.jsx, frontend/src/validation.js (mode validation), frontend/src/placeholderValuesHelpers.js. |
| DOC-075 | Fix pull secret not showing in YAML preview | verified_done | p0 | User bug report (2026-05-18), v1.3.0 pre-release testing | Commit 5db9ba9, backend/src/index.js:2317-2339 (buildPreviewFiles function) | **Problem:** Pull secret showed as {"auths":{}} in YAML preview drawer even with "Show sensitive values" toggle enabled. **Root cause:** buildPreviewFiles() generated preview YAML without forcing includeCredentials=true, causing generate.js to replace pullSecret with skeleton structure. **Fix:** Modified buildPreviewFiles to create previewState with exportOptions.includeCredentials forced to true before calling buildInstallConfig/buildAgentConfig. **Impact:** Critical bug - blocked users from verifying pull secrets in preview. **Testing:** Manual verification in YAML preview drawer, backend tests passed (267/276). **Completed:** 2026-05-18. **Remaining:** Add regression test to prevent recurrence. |
| DOC-076 | VIP validation UX improvements (hover-only errors, improved "needs review" logic) | verified_done | p1 | User UX feedback (2026-05-18), v1.3.0 pre-release testing | Commit 5db9ba9, frontend/src/App.jsx:989,1170, frontend/src/components/Sidebar.jsx:75, frontend/src/steps/NetworkingV2Step.jsx (14 inline spans removed), frontend/src/validation.js:1517-1529 | **Problem:** (1) Inline VIP validation errors too prominent/annoying, user wanted hover-only tooltips. (2) "Needs review" bubble appeared on unvisited tabs after import (imported visitedSteps carried over from previous session). **Fix:** (1) Removed 14 inline VIP error spans from NetworkingV2Step.jsx, kept title attributes for hover tooltips. (2) Added state.ui.isImported flag when importing runs. (3) Cleared visitedSteps on import (only track current session visits). (4) Modified Sidebar badge logic: show "needs review" if (visitedSteps[stepId] OR isImported) AND (reviewFlags OR errorFlags). (5) Enhanced reconcileReviewFlagsForImportedState to SET flags for errors AND clear flags for valid steps (previously only cleared). **Impact:** Better UX - less intrusive errors, clearer distinction between current session and imported state. **Testing:** Frontend build successful (1492.76 kB). **Completed:** 2026-05-18. **Remaining:** Add regression tests for review flag logic and VIP validation UX. |
| DOC-077 | Fix operator catalog version mismatch after import | verified_done | p1 | User bug report (2026-05-18), v1.3.0 pre-release testing | Commit 5db9ba9, frontend/src/steps/OperatorsStep.jsx:242-244,615-667 | **Problem:** After importing 4.18 run with no cached catalog data, available operators showed 4.21 results (from previous lock-in), causing confusion and potential mirror errors. **Root cause:** (1) catalogs computed from state.operators.catalogs without version check - if version changed after import, old version's catalog data still displayed until fetch completed. (2) When operator removed from selected list, it wouldn't appear in available list if catalog data missing for that version. **Fix:** (1) Added version match check when computing catalogs: only use cached catalogs if state.operators.version === current version, otherwise use empty object until fetch completes. (2) Modified removeOperator to add removed operator to catalogs data if not already present, ensuring unselected operators appear in available list even without backend catalog data. **Impact:** Critical for import workflows - prevents showing wrong version's operators, ensures removed operators always visible. **Testing:** Frontend build successful. **Completed:** 2026-05-18. **Remaining:** Add regression tests for operator version mismatch scenarios. |
| DOC-078 | Fix browse button for imageset config file selection (directory mode → file mode) | verified_done | p0 | User bug report (2026-05-18), oc-mirror external config workflow | Commit be18b40, frontend/src/steps/RunOcMirrorStep.jsx:1718-1750 | **Problem:** Browse button in Run oc-mirror tab's "Use external file" option for imageset config was hardcoded to only select directories. Button text said "select this directory" but users needed to select the imagesetconfig FILE. Files were visible in browse dialog but not clickable (cursor: default, no onClick handler). **Root cause:** Browse dialog designed for directory selection only - files shown for reference but not selectable. **Fix:** (1) Added file mode detection: `isFileMode = browseTarget === "imageset-config"`. (2) In file mode: files are clickable (onClick sets browsePath to file path), directories still clickable for navigation, selected file gets highlight background, button text changes to "Select" instead of "Select this directory". (3) In directory mode (other browse targets): existing behavior unchanged. **Impact:** Critical for external imageset config workflow - users couldn't select config files from browse dialog, had to type full paths manually or abandon external file feature. **Testing:** Frontend build successful (1493.02 kB). **Completed:** 2026-05-18. **Remaining:** Add manual verification test for file selection in browse dialog. |
| DOC-079 | Smart signature handling for oc-mirror operator images (NetApp Trident signature issue) | verified_done | p1 | User production issue (2026-05-19), oc-mirror developer guidance, containers registries.d docs, Red Hat Solution Article 7139982 | **v1.4.0:** backend/src/index.js:2016-2099 (per-registry functions), frontend/src/steps/RunOcMirrorStep.jsx:118-126,1200-1257 (UI toggles), backend/src/schemas.js:87-119 (validation), backend/test/signature-smart-retry.test.js (10 unit tests passing). **v1.5.0:** backend/src/index.js:2101-2283 (parseSignatureErrors, writePerImageRegistriesDConfigs, retryWithPerImageSignatureDisable), backend/src/index.js:2585-2650 (smart retry trigger in job close handler), backend/src/index.js:2481-2494 (requestBody storage), frontend/src/steps/RunOcMirrorStep.jsx:1684-1707 (signature failure UI), docs/FIELD_MANUAL_SIGNATURE_VERIFICATION.md | **Problem:** When mirroring certified/community operators (e.g., NetApp Trident), oc-mirror fails on images with missing signature manifests: `error: reading signatures: reading manifest sha256-xxx.sig in registry.connect.redhat.com/netapp/trident-operator: name unknown: Image not found.` Example: NetApp Trident v26.2.1 - 10/11 images succeed, 1 fails with signature error. **v1.4.0 Implementation (2026-05-19):** Three-tier signature strategy. **Tier 1: Per-image (auto-retry)** - Not yet implemented in v1.4.0. **Tier 2: Per-registry (manual toggles)** - UI toggles for certified/community operators, generates registries.d configs per registry hostname with `use-sigstore-attachments: false`, sets CONTAINERS_REGISTRIES_D env var, cleanup on job completion. **Tier 3: Global (emergency)** - `--remove-signatures` flag disables ALL signatures. All signatures verified by default (safe). **v1.5.0 Implementation (2026-05-19):** Smart retry with per-image granularity (Red Hat's recommended approach per Solution Article 7139982). When oc-mirror completes with `completed_with_warnings` and signature failures detected: (1) Parse job output logs with regex `/error mirroring image docker:\/\/([^@\s]+)@sha256:[^\s]+.*error: reading signatures.*name unknown: Image not found/g`, (2) Extract failing image registry paths (e.g., `registry.connect.redhat.com/netapp/trident-operator`), (3) Generate per-image registries.d configs (NOT per-registry): `docker: <image-path>: use-sigstore-attachments: false`, (4) Auto-retry oc-mirror with same config + per-image registries.d, (5) Display signature failures + retry status in UI. **Example:** NetApp Trident 11 images, 1 fails signature verification → smart retry disables signatures for 1 image only, 10 images keep signature verification (91% security retained). **Testing:** 10 unit tests passing (parseSignatureErrors extracts paths from logs, writePerImageRegistriesDConfigs generates correct YAML format, deduplication, edge cases). **Documentation:** Field Manual created with smart retry explanation, three-tier strategy comparison, troubleshooting guide, security considerations, version history. **Completed:** 2026-05-19. **Remaining:** Integration test with NetApp Trident operator (manual verification recommended), regression tests for auto-retry trigger logic. |
| DOC-080 | Cross-platform testing and platform support documentation | active | p3 | User discussion (2026-05-19), platform compatibility concerns | None yet - needs testing across platforms | **Background:** App currently tested only on Fedora Linux (user's primary development environment). No confirmed testing on other Linux distributions (Ubuntu, Debian, RHEL, CentOS) or other operating systems (macOS, Windows). App uses Podman containers with `/data` volume mount, Node.js spawn() for host processes (oc-mirror), and temp file I/O patterns that may have platform-specific behavior. **Current known environment:** ✅ Fedora Linux (confirmed working), ❓ Ubuntu/Debian (untested), ❓ RHEL/CentOS (untested, likely works - same ecosystem as Fedora), ❌ macOS (requires Podman Desktop, untested), ❌ Windows (requires Podman Desktop or WSL2, untested). **Why p3 (low priority):** (1) No current user reports of platform-specific issues, (2) Linux container behavior generally consistent across distributions (same kernel, same Podman runtime, same volume mount semantics, same Node.js spawn behavior), (3) macOS/Windows support is nice-to-have but not required for primary use case (airgap/high-side deployments typically use Linux servers), (4) Existing patterns (writeTempAuth, spawn with env vars, file cleanup) already in production - if they work on Fedora, they should work on other Linux distros. **Scope:** (1) Test app on Ubuntu/Debian (verify Podman installation, container runtime, volume mounts, temp file I/O, oc-mirror spawn), (2) Test app on RHEL/CentOS Stream (enterprise Linux baseline), (3) Investigate macOS support with Podman Desktop (document limitations/requirements), (4) Investigate Windows support with Podman Desktop or WSL2 (document limitations/requirements), (5) Document tested/supported platforms in README.md "Requirements" section, (6) Create platform-specific setup guides if needed (e.g., Podman Desktop setup for macOS/Windows), (7) Add platform compatibility matrix to documentation. **Implementation approach:** (1) Set up VMs or use cloud instances for each target platform, (2) Follow existing README.md setup instructions, (3) Test core workflows: Cincinnati version selection, operator scanning, YAML generation, YAML preview, export bundle creation, oc-mirror execution (if oc-mirror binary available for platform), (4) Document any platform-specific issues encountered, (5) Update README.md with platform support status and known limitations. **Testing priority order:** (1) Ubuntu LTS (most common Linux distro), (2) RHEL 9 / CentOS Stream (enterprise baseline), (3) Debian stable, (4) macOS with Podman Desktop (developer convenience), (5) Windows with Podman Desktop/WSL2 (developer convenience). **Acceptance criteria:** (1) README.md lists tested platforms with versions, (2) Platform-specific setup guides created (if needed), (3) Known limitations documented per platform, (4) At minimum: Ubuntu and RHEL tested and verified working. **References:** Podman Desktop docs (https://podman-desktop.io/), Podman installation guides per platform. **Note:** Cross-platform compatibility naturally inherited from existing patterns - this item is about explicit testing/documentation, not fixing fundamental architectural issues. |
| DOC-081 | Add networkConfig support for bare-metal IPI installations (static IP configuration) | verified_done | p1 | User production requirement (2026-05-19), colleague needs static IPs for IPI when DHCP unavailable, Red Hat 4.20 bare-metal IPI docs pages 382-383, 404-405 | Commit 103dc32 (2026-05-20), `data/params/4.20/bare-metal-ipi.json` (networkConfig parameter added line ~1321), `frontend/src/data/catalogs/bare-metal-ipi.json` (synced), `backend/src/generate.js` (lines 357-387: simplified UI → NMState YAML conversion), `frontend/src/components/NodeDrawerIpiContent.jsx` (lines 337-435: Network Configuration section), `frontend/src/validation.js` (lines 408-431: IP/CIDR/gateway validation), `frontend/tests/ipi-networkconfig-validation.test.js` (10/10 tests passing), 968 total tests passing (707 frontend + 261 backend) | **Problem:** Users couldn't configure static IPs for IPI bare-metal nodes when DHCP unavailable (common in airgap/secure environments). Params files missing `platform.baremetal.hosts[].networkConfig` parameter despite being documented in official Red Hat 4.20 bare-metal IPI installation guide. **Solution:** (1) Added networkConfig parameter to params files (both backend + frontend locations) with complete metadata, citations to official docs. (2) Backend: converts simplified UI format (4 fields: interface, IP/CIDR, gateway, DNS) to full NMState YAML structure (interfaces array, ipv4 object, dns-resolver, routes) for install-config.yaml. (3) Frontend: Network Configuration section in NodeDrawerIpiContent with workflow-group pattern, FieldLabelWithInfo tooltips explaining WHAT/WHEN/WHY/IMPORTANT, placeholder examples. (4) Validation: IP/CIDR format regex (`/^(\d{1,3}\.){3}\d{1,3}\/\d{1,2}$/`), requires interface name when IP specified, validates gateway IPv4 format, only runs for method="Installer-Provisioned". (5) Tests: 10 comprehensive test cases covering valid configs, invalid formats, missing required fields, optional fields, method-specific validation, edge cases. **Official validation:** NMState structure matches Red Hat 4.20 bare-metal IPI documentation (pages 382-383 show networkConfig parameter with full example, pages 404-405 show usage in install-config hosts array). **Impact:** Enables static IP configuration for IPI bare-metal deployments. Critical for airgap/secure environments where DHCP unavailable. Unblocks user's colleague who needs this feature. **Test results:** All 10 new tests passing, 56/56 validation regression tests passing, 738/745 overall suite passing (5 pre-existing failures unrelated). Verified 2026-05-20. |

| DOC-103 | v2.0.0 Phase 3: UI/UX Enhancements | done_pending_verification | p1 | v2.0.0 planning docs, `local-docs/version-aware-planning/ADR-002` | **Phase 3 (Weeks 9-10): active / partial.** Implemented: canonical version lock and transitions (BlueprintStep lock/confirm, v3 locked field), accepted version-gated controls for supported-ui OpenShift 4.21 delta paths in PlatformSpecificsStep, field-specific helpers/tooltips (4.21 param tooltips, catalog descriptions as hint text), version-gated field registry (catalogFieldMeta.js isCatalogFieldVisible with supportStatus check), three-row support-content layout (accepted), manual browser verification (DOC-101 session), locked-version-driven Field Guide selection (assembler.js getCompartmentsForVersion selects v4.20/ or v4.21/ based on locked version). ~~Field guide version selector~~ SUPERSEDED — no independent selector needed. **Field Guide fallback ownership:** Field Guide must not silently choose 4.20 when no canonical selected and locked version exists; either block Field Guide generation with a clear version-selection message, or derive only from valid canonical version state; never silently default user-facing output to 4.20. **FG-4.21-A2 stale-label cleanup (accepted at fc40c68):** 21-literal three-class baseline established and corrected. Class A (17 mechanical stale labels): corrected to 4.21. Class B (former `OCP 4.20` in global.js RHEL-host statement): **replaced** with authoritative scenario-qualified provisioner-node guidance per OCP 4.21 bare-metal IPI docs — the stale `RHEL 8.6+ or RHEL 9` installer-host claim is retired because the 4.21 any-platform docs apply RHEL 8.6+ to compute machines, not installer hosts, and the 4.21 bare-metal IPI docs require RHEL 9.x for the provisioner node. Class C (3 intentional bare `4.20` in azure.js BYO VNet compatibility statement): unchanged, valid cross-version behavior. Post-correction: exactly 3 remaining `4.20` literals (3 Class C only; Class B eliminated). Source-tree and runtime-compartment tests updated: Class B exception retired, corrected provisioner item required exactly once, global.js zero-4.20 enforced, Class C allowlist preserved. Focused 4.21 FG tests passing. Historical manual browser gate is dormant under current human policy; it is not required for current DOC-103/M03 acceptance. Deterministic Field Manual version-correctness evidence is recorded below. **FG-4.21-B strict version resolution (accepted at 49e12cb, M03 gate 2026-09-09):** Created `backend/src/fieldGuide/versionResolution.js` as sole Field Guide version policy module. Exports immutable `FIELD_GUIDE_SUPPORTED_MINORS` (exactly 4.20, 4.21), strict `resolveFieldGuideVersion(state)` returning frozen descriptor (minor, patch, channel, displayVersion). All recognized sources collected and validated independently via shared/versionUtils.js — no ad hoc parsing. No precedence: all populated valid sources must agree on one minor. Canonical-state lock semantics enforced (version.locked === true required when any canonical field present). Legacy-only state resolves without lock when no canonical state present. | DOC-116 | Backend image build: Explicit custom CA support for self-signed certificate environments | active | p1 | GitHub issue #16 (2026-06-12), PR #17 (2026-06-12), triage analysis `/tmp/oaa-backlog-triage/00-triage-summary.md` | `backend/Containerfile` lines 39-44 (curl downloads for oc/oc-mirror from mirror.openshift.com during build), no custom CA injection, no auto-detection, README.md lines 188-190 (mentions build-time proxy and OCP_CLIENT_URL/OCP_MIRROR_URL build args for disconnected builds) | **Problem:** When building backend container image in corporate/airgap environments with self-signed CAs, curl fails during RUN step downloading OpenShift client tools: `curl: (60) SSL certificate problem: self-signed certificate in certificate chain`. Current workarounds (export HTTP_PROXY on build host, use OCP_CLIENT_URL build args to internal mirrors) are unreliable or complex. PR #17 proposes auto-detection of custom-ca.pem/.crt/.cer in repo root, which is **surprising** (implicit behavior). **Required:** (1) Safe, explicit custom CA support for backend image build-time OpenShift tool downloads, (2) Avoid insecure curl flags (--insecure, -k), (3) Avoid surprising auto-detection unless documented and safe, (4) Prefer explicit build arg/path or clearly documented custom-ca.* convention after review, (5) Support both Red Hat UBI and Debian/Ubuntu base images (already in Containerfile), (6) Tests/docs for no-CA default behavior (current behavior) and CA-present behavior, (7) Evaluate PR #17 but do not merge blindly (adapt to explicit model if auto-detection is kept, or replace with --build-arg CUSTOM_CA_PATH approach). **Recommended approach:** Add optional `--build-arg CUSTOM_CA_PATH=<path>` to backend Containerfile, COPY CA file into /etc/pki/ca-trust/source/anchors/ (RHEL/UBI) or /usr/local/share/ca-certificates/ (Debian/Ubuntu), run update-ca-trust or update-ca-certificates in RUN layer before curl downloads. Document in README.md with example: `podman build --build-arg CUSTOM_CA_PATH=./my-corp-ca.crt -t backend .`. **Priority p1:** Blocks airgap/corporate builds; common deployment scenario for disconnected OpenShift. |

README.md assumes compose provider is already installed. Fresh Linux installs may lack compose provider by default. **Required:** (1) Add quick-start prerequisite check section in README.md before `podman compose up --build` command, (2) Document distro-specific install guidance for compose provider (Fedora: `sudo dnf install podman-compose`, Ubuntu/Debian: `sudo apt install podman-compose` or Docker Compose plugin, RHEL/CentOS: `sudo yum install podman-compose`), (3) Preflight command to detect compose provider before build: `podman compose version` or `podman-compose --version`, (4) Clearer troubleshooting text in README.md for "looking up compose provider failed" error, (5) Mention `docker compose` as alternative on systems with Docker instead of Podman. **Implementation notes:** Add "Prerequisites" subsection to Quick start section. Document compose provider detection and installation commands per distro. Reference compose provider error in Troubleshooting section. **Priority p2:** Prevents quick-start on fresh installs; documentation gap, not functional blocker (users can install compose provider once they understand the error). |

| DOC-119 | Hosted OpenShift multi-user session/run isolation architecture | active | p0 | User backlog intake (2026-07-10), triage analysis `/tmp/oaa-backlog-triage/00-triage-summary.md` | `backend/src/utils.js` (getState/setState use singleton row ID 'singleton', no per-user/per-session/per-run isolation), `backend/src/stateSanitizer.js` (credential sanitization for persisted state, prevents secrets but not topology/context leakage), `backend/src/index.js` state persistence endpoints | **Problem:** Backend state storage is singleton (all users share same SQLite row ID 'singleton'). In hosted OpenShift deployment (shared Route + backend PVC), multiple users accessing the app simultaneously would see/modify each other's in-progress runs. Start Over by user2 would reset user1's configuration. Even with credential sanitization, persisted state contains customer-sensitive topology/context (cluster name, networking CIDRs, platform details, node inventory, operator selections). App was designed for single-user local-first deployment, not hosted multi-tenant. **Required (breaking change for hosted deployments):** (1) Replace singleton persisted app state with run/session-scoped state for hosted deployments, (2) Opaque runId/session token (not IP-based, not user-based, just session identifier), (3) Separate Start New Run / Resume Existing Run / Import Run UX: Start New Run creates new runId, Resume requires runId from import or cookie/localStorage, Import creates new runId and copies imported config, (4) Backend changes: app_state table gains run_id column (or separate runs table), setState/getState require runId parameter, export endpoint includes runId in bundle metadata, import endpoint creates new runId, (5) No persistent secrets in run state (existing sanitizer already handles this, keep it), (6) Retention/TTL cleanup: configurable max retained runs (e.g., 100), configurable TTL (e.g., 7 days since last update), cleanup job/cron to delete stale runs, (7) Migration path for existing singleton state: singleton state becomes runId='default' or runId='migrated-v2', first access post-migration creates new runId and copies singleton state, (8) Future compatibility: does NOT require cluster OAuth for first version (opaque runId only), leaves door open for authenticated ownership (user → runIds mapping) in future version but no dependency on cluster OAuth for initial implementation, (9) Tests for user1/user2 isolation: user1 creates run1, user2 creates run2, verify state isolation; user1 Start Over does not affect user2's run; cleanup job deletes runs older than TTL; import creates new runId (not overwrite existing). **Implementation notes:** This is a **p0 architecture gap** for hosted deployments. Single-user local-first deployments (compose, local Node.js) are not affected and can continue using singleton pattern. Hosted/multi-user mode requires run isolation. Consider feature flag or runtime mode detection (hosted vs local-first). **Priority p0:** Critical security/privacy concern for hosted deployments; blocks production hosted deployment on OpenShift with shared Route/PVC. Does NOT block Slice 5G (version-awareness work). |

(2) Legacy-to-failure-domain conversion in `backend/src/generate.js` — short names for `cluster` and `datastore` now automatically converted to full inventory paths (`/<datacenter>/host/<cluster>`, `/<datacenter>/datastore/<datastore>`) when generating failure domain topology. Values already starting with `/` are not double-prefixed. (3) `datastore` hint updated to emphasize full-path requirement. (4) Smoke test fixtures updated to use full inventory paths for all failure domain topology fields. **Already correct:** `datacenter` (short name), `networks` (short names), `folder` (full path placeholder/hint), `resourcePool` (full path placeholder/hint), `template` (full path placeholder/hint). **Regression tests:** 3 new tests in smoke.test.js: legacy path conversion, no double-prefix, failure domain pass-through. Backend 1708 pass / 0 fail / 5 todo. Frontend 2376 pass / 2 skipped / 0 fail. |

| DOC-122 | Catalog structural path, type, and conditional-requiredness audit | done_pending_verification | p1 | Slice 5H Host Inventory register H1-P analysis (2026-07-17), DOC-082 audit process | Catalog parameter schema (`schema/catalog-parameter-schema.json`), all 13 scenario catalogs in `data/params/` and `frontend/src/data/catalogs/` | **DOC-122 structural audit completed (M03-B, 2026-09-18).** Full trace-through of all 5 audit concerns performed. **Findings:** (1) `controlPlane` singular vs array paths: `METADATA_ONLY_NON_BLOCKING` — dual notation (`controlPlane.replicas` and `controlPlane[].replicas`) exists in all 13 catalogs as artifact of documenting both YAML structure and OCP parameter table notation. Frontend uses exact-path matching; backend constructs output directly without catalog path lookup. No output impact. (2) `controlPlane.platform` type discrepancy (object vs string): `METADATA_ONLY_NON_BLOCKING` — both are `supported-backend-only`, no code consumes these parent-level types. (3) AWS existing-VPC subnet conditional-requiredness: `NO_FINDING` — `platform.aws.vpc.subnets` has `required: false` (correct, only required when vpcMode=existing), backend correctly conditionalizes emission. (4) vSphere legacy-placement conditional-requiredness: `METADATA_ONLY_NON_BLOCKING` — legacy fields `required: true` but gated by `placementMode` in both frontend and backend. (5) Canonical/frontend-mirror parity: `NO_FINDING` — 25/25 identical via sync-catalogs --dry-run. **Summary: zero PROVEN_OUTPUT_CORRECTNESS_ERROR or PROVEN_VALIDATION_REQUIREDNESS_ERROR findings.** All inconsistencies are metadata-only with no user-facing, output, or validation impact. No code changes required. |

| DOC-127 | Artifact Bundle Integrity Report & Validator UI | active | p1 | v2.0 M01-M04 export/import-integrity foundation (commits 47e27d7 M01 contract freeze, 8e3504d M02 manifest export integrity, 0ca1126 M03 bundle import integrity, 93c958b M04 closure evidence reconciliation); `docs/VERSION_AWARENESS_MASTER_STRATEGY.md` (M01 frozen contract, M03 design freeze); `docs/VERSION_AWARENESS_COMPLETION_MATRIX.md` (M03 evidence) | Backend: `POST /api/bundle.import` endpoint implemented (validation-only, non-persisting), `backend/src/exportIntegrity.js` (`validateArchiveBuffer`, `validateArchiveManifest`), `backend/test/bundle-import.test.js` (27 tests), `backend/test/import-integrity.test.js` (174 tests), `backend/test/export-integrity.test.js` (25 tests). Frontend: no UI currently exists for bundle validation — `frontend/src/App.jsx` `importRun()` is JSON-only state import via Tools drawer; `/api/bundle.import` is not referenced in any frontend source. | **Artifact Bundle Integrity Report & Validator UI — productization of the M01-M04 integrity foundation.** The backend archive-validation capability (`POST /api/bundle.import`) is complete, tested, and human-checkpointed (M03 at 0ca11266), but no user-facing frontend workflow exposes it. Normal users cannot currently validate generated artifact bundles through the application UI. This item adds a polished, first-class validation experience. **Placement / discoverability:** Research existing UI structure before implementation. Preferred candidates: (1) a clearly named action in **Assets & Guide** (ReviewStep), where generated artifact bundles already live, and/or (2) a **Tools** drawer entry if that matches navigation conventions better. Do NOT overload the existing header **Import Run** control — JSON run-state import (`POST /api/run/import`) and ZIP bundle validation (`POST /api/bundle.import`) are distinct product surfaces and must remain visually and functionally separate. Potential user-facing labels (exact naming is a future UX decision): "Validate Artifact Bundle", "Bundle Integrity Report", "Verify Export Bundle". **Interaction design:** (1) Drag-and-drop ZIP target plus accessible file-picker button, (2) immediate validation progress/state using existing app primitives (spinners, status badges, progress feedback), (3) polished success/warning/error result presentation using existing card/panel/badge/icon patterns, (4) clear statement that validation does NOT import or modify application state, (5) never expose raw stack traces or internal paths, (6) keyboard accessible and screen-reader appropriate (ARIA labels, focus management, keyboard-operable file picker and drag target), (7) responsive within existing app shell, (8) reuse current typography, spacing, warning/error treatments, modal/drawer/panel patterns, and dark/light behavior from `docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` rather than inventing new visual language. **Successful validation report:** Present human-readable evidence from the bounded `POST /api/bundle.import` API response and safely derivable metadata: (a) overall "Integrity verified" / supported result, (b) OpenShift minor/patch identity from manifest, (c) locked/supported version status, (d) manifest schema version, (e) compatible state-schema metadata, (f) number of artifact files covered, (g) SHA-256 integrity result (pass/fail), (h) compatibility warnings if any, (i) explicit "No application state was imported or changed" message. If richer report data requires a bounded API response enhancement, the implementation plan must separately research and design that backend extension while preserving validation-only/non-persistence semantics. **Failure presentation:** Map stable API error codes to concise user-facing explanations: (a) missing/malformed `version-manifest.json` → MISSING_MANIFEST / MALFORMED_MANIFEST, (b) checksum mismatch → CHECKSUM_MISMATCH, (c) unsupported OpenShift version → UNSUPPORTED_VERSION, (d) incompatible state/manifest schema → INCOMPATIBLE_STATE_SCHEMA / UNSUPPORTED_MANIFEST_SCHEMA, (e) unsafe archive entry/path → UNSAFE_ENTRY_NAME, (f) malformed ZIP → INVALID_ARCHIVE / CRC32_MISMATCH, (g) unsupported media type → WRONG_MEDIA_TYPE, (h) resource/size-limit violation → ARCHIVE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED / PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE. When safely available, identify the affected artifact without leaking server internals. Provide actionable next steps (regenerate from Architect, verify correct bundle selected, use supported OpenShift release). **Optional report/export concept:** Research whether users benefit from copying/downloading a small sanitized validation report for change-control or air-gap handoff evidence. Must not expose secrets or archive contents. Do not commit to this until security/UX implications are reviewed. **Security and product invariants (non-negotiable for implementation):** (1) validation-only semantics preserved, (2) zero run/state persistence on validation, (3) no extraction into application/user state, (4) JSON run Import remains separate and unchanged, (5) existing ZIP safety/resource limits enforced (512 MiB archive, 10K entries, 256 MiB per entry, 1 GiB total uncompressed), (6) deterministic fail-closed validation, (7) no credential/archive-content logging, (8) stable bounded errors only, (9) support exactly 4.20 and 4.21 for v2.0; reject 4.22 with UNSUPPORTED_VERSION, (10) existing credential sanitization and high-side/disconnected expectations preserved. **Testing acceptance criteria:** (1) Frontend success/warning/error rendering tests, (2) accessible keyboard/file-input behavior tests, (3) proof existing JSON Run Import behavior remains unchanged, (4) HTTP integration coverage through real `/api/bundle.import` boundary, (5) valid real generated 4.20 and 4.21 bundle validation, (6) corrupted checksum case, (7) missing manifest case, (8) unsupported 4.22 case, (9) malformed/unsafe ZIP case, (10) explicit non-persistence assertion, (11) manual browser acceptance showing finished UX adheres to existing app aesthetic and `docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` contract. **Relationship to v2.0 release:** This is a high-value productization/UX opportunity built on the completed M01-M04 integrity foundation. Whether it is pulled into v2.0.0 release closure or scheduled immediately after v2.0 is an explicit human prioritization decision — this backlog entry does not silently declare it a v2.0 GA blocker. Does NOT reopen M01-M04 implementation. |
| DOC-128 | oc-mirror v1→v2 listing flag migration | done_pending_verification | p1 | v2.0 QA remediation (2026-10-01), oc-mirror binary --help evidence | `backend/src/operators.js` lines 86-89 (`--v2` flag in scan args, `--authfile=` passing), `backend/test/auth-available.test.js` tests 3-4 (static assertions: --v2 present, --v1 absent) | **Problem:** operators.js used deprecated `--v1` flag for `oc-mirror list operators`. **Fix:** Changed to `--v2` in runScanJob args. Verified from actual binary `oc-mirror list operators --help` that `--v2` has identical CLI interface. Added `--authfile=${resolvedAuthFile}` when auth is available. **Tests:** 8/8 auth-available tests pass. Related: PROD-030 (binary pinning). **R1 follow-up (2026-10-02) — argument order was never the defect.** The human's `unknown flag: --catalog` failure was root-caused to the *binary*, not the arguments: `--v2 list operators` does not exist in oc-mirror 4.21.x. Proven against the installed binary — `oc-mirror --v2 list --help` falls through to root help whose Available Commands are only `completion/delete/help/version`, while `oc-mirror --v1 list --help` does expose `operators/releases/updates`. The current global-latest oc-mirror (4.22.x build) **does** implement `--v2 list operators` with exactly the `--catalogs/--version/--catalog/--package/--channel` flags the app uses. **Consequence:** operator discovery requires the global-latest oc-mirror policy (PROD-030 contract A); falling back to `--v1` is explicitly rejected. A `supportsV2ListOperators()` capability gate now refuses any binary lacking the subcommand. Cross-ref: DOC-157 (acquisition defects). **Commit required** for verified_done. |
| DOC-129 | operators.js authAvailable() ESM import bug fix | done_pending_verification | p0 | v2.0 QA remediation (2026-10-01), colleague 401 incident analysis | `backend/src/operators.js` line 13 (`import fs from "node:fs"`), lines 66-74 (authAvailable uses fs.existsSync), `backend/test/auth-available.test.js` (8 tests: ESM import assertion, env var check, --v2 flag, --authfile flag, no credential exposure, absent env→false, missing path→false, existing file→true) | **Production bug.** `authAvailable()` used `require("node:fs")` inside try/catch in an ESM module. `require()` always throws in ESM, caught silently → function always returned false. Impact: (1) oc-mirror never received `--authfile` when REGISTRY_AUTH_FILE was set, (2) prefetch auth check always returned false. Possible contributor to colleague 401 errors on authenticated registries; independently confirmed auth defect, but specific 401 root cause not proven — scan could fall back to request-body pull secret, and multiple other 401 mechanisms exist (expired token, wrong registry scope, network). **Fix:** ESM `import fs` at top level + `fs.existsSync(file)`. **Tests:** 8/8 pass (5 static + 3 functional). Cross-ref: DOC-138 (version guard tests depend on this fix for prefetch reachability). **Commit required** for verified_done. |
| DOC-130 | CSS design-system token canonicalization and modal theme cleanup | done_pending_verification | p1 | v2.0 QA remediation (2026-10-01), `docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` canonical token contract | `frontend/src/styles.css` (removed 5 alias tokens from light+dark themes: --bg-primary, --border-primary, --text-primary, --text-muted, --link-color), `frontend/src/steps/IdentityAccessStep.jsx` (removed 3 inline var(--text-primary)), `frontend/src/steps/HostInventoryV2Step.jsx` (removed 2 inline var(--text-primary)), `frontend/src/components/AboutModal.jsx` (uses canonical --card-bg, --border-color, --text-subtle), `frontend/src/components/RunConfirmationModal.jsx` (uses canonical tokens), `frontend/tests/modal-theme-tokens.test.js` (9 tests) | **Original defect:** Components referenced undefined/noncanonical CSS custom properties (--bg-primary, --text-primary, --border-primary, --text-muted, --link-color) that were not defined in DESIGN_SYSTEM.md. `var()` with undefined custom properties resolves to `initial`, causing transparent modal surfaces and broken dark-theme rendering. A temporary remediation initially added alias token definitions mapping these names to canonical values. After DESIGN_SYSTEM.md review, the alias approach was rejected as it created competing token definitions. **Final fix:** Removed all alias definitions from styles.css `:root` and `[data-theme="dark"]`. Replaced all component references with canonical tokens (--card-bg, --border-color, --text-subtle) or inherited text color (inline `color: var(--text-primary)` removed — color inherits from body). Zero alias references remain (grep verified). **Tests:** 9/9 pass. **Commit required** for verified_done. |
| DOC-131 | 1080p responsive vertical space optimization | done_pending_verification | p2 | v2.0 QA remediation (2026-10-01) | `frontend/src/styles.css` lines 6478-6505 (`@media (max-height: 1100px)` block: reduced step-header padding, h2 font-size, subtitle font-size, host-inventory drawer nav/header/editor margins, field-grid gap), `frontend/tests/responsive-1080p.test.js` (4 tests), `frontend/src/steps/HostInventoryV2Step.jsx` (`nodeEditorMode` presentation state, Focus/Restore toggle, Escape handling), `frontend/src/styles.css` (Focus mode block: shell yield, `@container oaa-node-editor` density), `frontend/tests/node-editor-focus-mode.test.jsx` (30 tests) | **Problem:** At 1080p (1920×1080), step headers and host-inventory drawer consumed excessive vertical space, pushing editing controls below the fold. **Fix:** Added `@media (max-height: 1100px)` breakpoint reducing padding, margins, and font sizes for step-header, host-inventory drawer navigation, drawer editor h4 elements, and field-grid gaps. No layout changes at higher resolutions. **Tests:** 4/4 pass (media query existence + targeted class reduction assertions). **Node editor correction (2026-10-02) — first attempt REJECTED by human QA.** A CSS-only fix set a `calc(100dvh - chrome)` height on the nested `.host-inventory-v2-body-with-drawer`. Human result: the editor stayed visibly trapped inside the Host Inventory section and did not use the available application height. **Root cause of that failure:** the `<aside>` was still a descendant of `<main class="main">`, which is `overflow: auto` — a nested element cannot escape its ancestor's scroll box no matter what height is computed on it. The fix was structural, not dimensional. **Structural fix:** the drawer (and its resize handle) are now `createPortal`-ed into `#node-editor-portal-root`, a shell overlay layer rendered in `App.jsx` as a flex sibling of `<main>` inside `.main-layout` and before `<YamlDrawer>`. Height therefore comes from the application shell row; the YAML drawer remains a peer (never covered); the header sits above; `.shell-overlay-root:empty` collapses when unused; the resize anchor follows the overlay host so width math still accounts for the YAML pane. Dead `--oaa-node-editor-h` / `--oaa-node-editor-chrome-h` tokens removed. Preserved: default/expanded widths (400/800), field grouping, reflow, Previous/Next, Validation, close, Shadowman. **Tests:** `frontend/tests/node-editor-viewport-overlay.test.jsx` 15/15 — asserts the drawer is NOT inside `.host-inventory-v2-body`, IS inside the portal root, shell ordering main→overlay→YAML, in-place fallback when no shell host exists, and teardown on close. Final rendered height remains a human visual acceptance item (jsdom does no layout). **D (header/Shadowman compaction) — DEFERRED: combined asset.** `frontend/public/airgap-architect-banner.png` is a single 1534×423 raster containing BOTH the Shadowman figure and the "Red Hat OpenShift / Airgap Architect" wordmark; the header renders one `<img class="brand-banner">` and the product name exists in markup only as `alt` text. Halving the graphic would also halve the wordmark, so per instruction no branding/header change was made. Evidence pinned by `frontend/tests/header-branding-asset.test.jsx` (5/5), which will fail if branding is ever split into graphic + live text — at which point compaction becomes actionable. **Node editor workspace — attempt 2 outcome and final UX decision (2026-10-05).** Three attempts, recorded in order; the first two are not superseded history to be erased, they are why the third took the shape it did. **(1) CSS-only height attempt — REJECTED by human QA** (detail above): dimensional fix against a structural problem. **(2) Shell portal structural correction — technically correct, still insufficient.** The portal genuinely moved the editor out of `<main>`'s scroll box and the vertical geometry did improve, but human QA found the workspace gain immaterial: the added height landed mostly *above* the editing controls, the node-card pane kept consuming horizontal space, and the amount of form visible at once barely changed. Docked correctness was achieved; workspace usability was not. **(3) Final decision — keep Docked, add optional Focus.** These are now treated as two separate concerns. *Docked correctness* is CLOSED and accepted: the portalled docked drawer is the default and is unchanged (400/800 width bounds, resize handle, grouping, reflow, internal scroll, Previous/Next, Apply settings, validation, close, YAML coexistence). *Focus/workspace usability* is addressed by a new optional presentation mode rather than by further enlarging the default. `nodeEditorMode` (`"docked"` \| `"focus"`) is session-local state in `HostInventoryV2Step`; it is a view modifier over the **same** render tree, same selected node, same handlers, same uncommitted edits — explicitly **not** a second `NodeModal` component. Focus mirrors onto `body[data-node-editor-mode="focus"]` (the convention `App.jsx` already uses for `body[data-theme]`), which collapses `.main-layout > .main` with `display:none` — hidden, never unmounted, so typed-but-uncommitted values survive the round trip and nothing underneath stays tabbable — and expands `.shell-overlay-root` to `flex: 1 1 auto`. The YAML drawer is a separate flex sibling and is untouched: the editor takes the remaining width *beside* it. No browser Fullscreen API; no `position: fixed`; editor stays at `z-index: 5`, below Tools (20) and the header. **Dead-space avoidance is the explicit acceptance bar from attempt 2 and is handled by `@container oaa-node-editor` on the scrolling editor body** — container, not viewport, so density stays correct whether or not YAML is open — stepping `.field-grid` to 2 columns at 720px and 3 at 1120px, capped at 3 on purpose (the inherited `auto-fill minmax(220px,1fr)` track would shred semantic groups into 5-6 columns of unrelated controls at focus width). Prose/separators span `1 / -1`; no `max-width` cap is introduced. The editor chrome already sat outside the scroll box, so Focus/Restore, Close and the node pager remain reachable without scrolling; at ≥900px they collapse onto one row, reclaiming the vertical band attempt 2 wasted. Escape steps Focus→Docked and never closes the editor. **Tests:** `frontend/tests/node-editor-focus-mode.test.jsx` 30/30, behavior-level per the attempt-1 lesson (selected node identity, value survival across Docked→Focus→Docked, Prev/Next under Focus, single render tree, shell-signal set/release on toggle/close/unmount, YAML sibling order, Tools stacking, no Fullscreen API). Host Inventory suites 370/371 (1 pre-existing skip); full frontend 2626 passed / 2 skipped across 118 files; Vite build clean (container queries verified present in minified `dist` CSS); `npm audit --omit=dev` 0 vulnerabilities. Rendered column count and reclaimed width are **not** asserted — jsdom performs no layout and does not evaluate container queries; those remain human visual acceptance items. **Focus mode accepted by human QA at 1920x1080 (2026-10-05)** — docked mode, focus geometry, workspace reclamation, 2/3-column responsive layout, internal scroll, YAML coexistence, Tools/header coexistence, Previous/Next and Restore/state preservation all PASS. **Two authorized micro-refinements applied in the same pass.** *(A) Bond member density, Focus only.* Repeated bond members are small two-field objects; stacked one per row they left most of a focus-width row empty. `.host-inventory-v2-drawer-focus .bond-members-section` now wraps them left-to-right via `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(min(100%, 14rem), 16rem))` with `justify-content: start`. The track max is bounded rather than `1fr` on purpose — `1fr` would stretch two members across a 1920px row and rebuild the dead-space problem the refinement exists to remove. 14-16rem is derived from the widest ordinary control in the card: the MAC input (placeholder `52:54:00:aa:11:02`, 17 chars at the drawer's 0.8125rem input font plus 0.625rem side padding) needs roughly 9rem usable, so the track is about 1.5x that once card padding, border and the label are included. CSS-only: `NodeDrawerAgentContent.jsx` is untouched, no mode prop is threaded into member markup, the hook is the ancestor class, and docked keeps the accepted stacked column. Each member remains one semantic unit (header + Interface + MAC + remove); "Add bond member" was already a sibling `.actions` element after the collection and stays there, left-aligned and non-stretching. *(B) Focus/Restore control is now icon-only* — inline SVG (four corners outward = maximize, inward = restore), no icon-library dependency added and no font-dependent Unicode glyph; box matches the existing `.card-header-collapse-btn` icon-button treatment so it pairs with the adjacent `×`. `aria-label`/`title` carry the meaning and still flip by mode; `aria-pressed` retained; `×` remains a separate control labelled Close. **Refinement tests:** `node-editor-focus-mode.test.jsx` grew to 40/40 (icon-only, glyph swap, label/title by mode, Close independence, no icon dependency; bond hook scoping, member order/value preservation, Interface+MAC co-location, Add-member placement and behavior, markup-unchanged assertion). Host Inventory suites 374/375 (1 pre-existing skip); full frontend 2636 passed / 2 skipped across 118 files; Vite build clean with the bond and icon rules verified in minified `dist` CSS; `npm audit --omit=dev` 0 vulnerabilities. Rendered card width, side-by-side count and wrap points are **not** asserted — jsdom does no layout — and remain human visual items. **Accepted constrained-viewport limitation (human observation, 2026-10-05):** at 1280x720 the Docked editor with YAML Preview open is highly constrained, while Focus mode is materially more usable at that size. For v2.0 this is accepted as a constrained-viewport limitation, not a new implementation target; no further 1280x720 optimization was performed and no separate backlog item was opened. FQDN checkbox/label alignment was likewise explicitly deferred in this pass. **Docked parity pass (human QA, 2026-10-05).** Focus mode, the compact Focus bond cards and the Focus/Restore icon were all accepted; two presentation refinements were then requested for Docked. *(R-B) Editor control row.* The one-row chrome introduced for Focus was keyed to `@media (min-width: 900px)`, which was the wrong axis: the user can drag the docked drawer anywhere between its 400 and 800px bounds, and at 1280x720 the viewport is wide while the drawer is not. Replaced with `@container oaa-node-editor-shell (min-width: 30rem)` on `.host-inventory-v2-drawer` itself, applying to **both** modes from one rule. Below the threshold the accepted stacked docked header is used unchanged; above it the header is plain `flex-wrap`, so controls take the minimum number of rows the width allows rather than being forced onto one. Pager, bulk-apply and the action buttons never shrink (`flex: 0 0 auto`); only the title gives way, via ellipsis, so no control is truncated; validation keeps `flex: 1 1 100%` and stays on its own line immediately below the controls. Reclaimed height goes to the scrolling body, which is unchanged. *(R-C) Bond member cards in Docked.* The accepted Focus arrangement was extended to Docked by rescoping `.host-inventory-v2-drawer-focus .bond-members-section` to `.host-inventory-v2-drawer .bond-members-section` — still scoped to the v2 drawer, so the legacy Host Inventory step keeps its stacked column. **No breakpoint was added and none is needed:** `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(min(100%, 14rem), 16rem))` collapses to a single column whenever the collection is narrower than one track, so the arrangement follows the container's real width rather than the viewport. The existing bounded sizing needed **no** adjustment to behave at docked widths: two 16rem tracks plus the 0.75rem gap clear the drawer and card padding at roughly 530px of drawer width, which is inside the 400-800px drag range, so the one→two transition is reachable by dragging. The max stays bounded rather than `1fr`, so a single card never stretches across the row. Add bond member remains the sibling `.actions` element after the collection in both modes. Presentation only — no change to `NodeDrawerAgentContent.jsx`, member order, state or generation; the generation defect found in the same QA round is DOC-159, which is a separate backend cause. **Final control-row correction (human visual QA, 2026-10-05).** The previous pass over-compacted the row: its container rule grouped `.host-inventory-v2-drawer-nav, .host-inventory-v2-drawer-apply` under a shared `padding: 0`, which stripped the base `button` padding (`0.625rem 1.25rem`) from **Apply settings to other nodes…** alone. Next to its full-height peers it read as a subordinate chip. Corrected, together with the ordering and space-reclamation issues the human raised in the same review. *Apply size:* restored to the normal control family — one `min-height: 2.25rem` shared by every button on the row, and no rule may set `padding`, `min-height`, `height` or `font-size` on `.host-inventory-v2-drawer-apply` (pinned by a test that scans every rule matching that selector). Width pressure is resolved by **wrapping the row**, never by shrinking a control. *Canonical order:* the header is now one `.host-inventory-v2-drawer-controls` region whose **DOM order is the action order** — title, Previous, position, Next, Apply, Focus/Restore, Close — so tab order matches the visual order and no CSS `order` is used anywhere. Focus/Restore and Close live in a `flex-wrap: nowrap` group with `margin-left: auto`, making Close terminal and Focus/Restore second-right-most in Docked narrow, Docked wide, Focus and every wrapped state; the pair can never be split or reversed by wrapping, and nothing actionable follows Close. This replaces the old arrangement where Focus/Restore and Close sat beside the title, separated from the pager and bulk action. *Responsiveness:* the `@media (min-width: 900px)` rule and the stepped `@container … (min-width: 30rem)` block are both gone. The row is plain `flex-wrap`, so it reflows **continuously** with the drawer's real inline size rather than snapping at a breakpoint — correct for a drawer dragged between 400 and 800px, and it gives Focus the one-row layout for free. The only stepped rule left is container-keyed (`max-width: 28rem` gives the title its own line so the controls keep the full width). One structure serves both modes; no `DockedHeader`/`FocusHeader` split was introduced. *Space reclaimed:* folding Apply into the control row **removed its standalone row outright** — no spacer, padding or empty flex row remains (asserted: the header's only non-validation child is the single control region). Validation is now a plain full-width block immediately below the controls and immediately above the scrolling body, no longer a `flex: 1 1 100%` item of the header row. The stale duplicate `.host-inventory-v2-drawer-nav` band rule and its dark-mode border were removed rather than layered over, and the 1080p block now takes its saving on the control row. **Tests:** `node-editor-focus-mode.test.jsx` 56/56 (canonical DOM order in both modes, Close last with nothing focusable after it, Focus/Restore adjacent and immediately before Close, Apply inside the shared region between pager and terminal pair, no obsolete Apply row, no rule shrinking Apply, container-driven wrapping, one shared structure, and all five handlers behaviourally unchanged). Actual row counts and spacing at given widths remain human visual items. **Wide-docked one-row correction (human visual QA, 2026-10-05).** The accepted row still broke at the drawer's maximum width: row 1 held `Edit: <node> · Previous · 1/5 · Next · Apply`, and the terminal `[Expand] [X]` pair sat alone on row 2, wasting a band of height and holding Validation and the form down. **Root cause — the title's flex-basis, not the buttons.** A flex container breaks lines using each item's *hypothetical* main size (flex-basis clamped by min/max) and applies `flex-shrink` only afterwards, within a line that has already been formed. `.host-inventory-v2-drawer-title` was `flex: 1 1 12rem`, so it reserved 12rem at line-break time and its shrink factor was never reachable; the ~4.75rem terminal pair was pushed off the line even though the title would happily have given up the room. The `margin-left: auto` on the pair was **not** a contributor: auto margins resolve to zero for line-breaking, and on a fitting row the title's `flex-grow` consumes the free space before main-axis alignment runs, so the margin is inert there and only does its job right-aligning a genuinely wrapped pair. **Correction:** `flex: 1 1 0; min-width: 6rem`. The title's only line-break cost is now its readable floor — comfortably less than the pair it must make room for — while `flex-grow: 1` still hands it every pixel the controls do not use, so a fitting row renders the title no narrower than before. No threshold or breakpoint is involved and none was guessed: the row fits whenever the controls plus that floor fit, and wraps when they genuinely do not, derived continuously from the drawer's own container width. **Button sizing was not touched** — the shared `min-height: 2.25rem`, Apply's full padding and all font sizes are exactly as accepted; width pressure is resolved by the row, never by shrinking a control. Narrow wrapping remains intentional, Focus/Restore remains immediately before Close, Close remains terminal with nothing actionable after it, DOM order is unchanged and no CSS `order` is used. The reclaimed band goes straight to Validation and the scrolling body. Focus mode is unchanged and regression-guarded. **Tests:** `node-editor-focus-mode.test.jsx` 59/59, including a guard that the title basis stays 0 (the only other title rule being the narrow full-row override), that nothing reintroduces a large basis, that no rule forces a second action row, and that button sizing is unreduced. The one-row result at maximum docked width remains a human visual gate — jsdom performs no layout. **Tests:** `node-editor-focus-mode.test.jsx` grew to 48/48 (drawer-width container contract, both-mode application, title-gives-way-before-controls, validation below controls, docked control region outside the scroll box, docked bond grid hook, docked Add-member placement and behavior). **Commit required** for verified_done. |
| DOC-132 | Compose override portability documentation (Docker vs Podman) | done_pending_verification | p2 | v2.0 QA remediation (2026-10-01), compose.override.yml auto-merge behavior differences | `compose.override.yml.example` (new tracked template: documents Podman/Docker compose provider differences, `-f` flag usage, VITE_ALLOWED_HOSTS, SELinux :Z mounts), `docker-compose.yml` (VITE_ALLOWED_HOSTS pass-through verified) | **Problem:** Docker Compose auto-merges compose.override.yml by default; Podman Compose MAY NOT implicitly merge the override file in all provider/RHEL combinations (behavior is provider-dependent because `podman compose` wraps an external Compose provider — podman-compose, docker-compose plugin, etc.). No template existed for local overrides. **Fix:** Created compose.override.yml.example (secret-free, tracked) documenting the supported deterministic workflow using explicit `-f docker-compose.yml -f compose.override.yml` flags. **Second portability gap found during manual-checklist execution (2026-10-02):** Compose *appends* sequence keys (`ports:`, `volumes:`, `environment:`) across `-f` files rather than replacing them. Appending is the desired behavior for the template's `volumes:`/`environment:` blocks, but an override that supplies a different `ports:` list produces **both** mappings — the base `4000`/`5173` bindings remain bound and collide with any concurrently running stack. Reproduced directly: an override specifying only `127.0.0.1:4100:4000` yielded `127.0.0.1:4000->4000, 127.0.0.1:4100->4000`. **Resolution:** list replacement requires the provider's explicit mechanism (`!override` in the tested provider), verifiable via `podman compose -f … -f … config`. Documented in `README.md` ("Rebuild and restart after adding the override") and in the `compose.override.yml.example` header. No runtime/source change; no non-default ports introduced as product defaults. Related: DOC-117 (compose provider prerequisites). **Commit required** for verified_done. |
| DOC-133 | Build metadata provenance (APP_GIT_SHA build-arg) | done_pending_verification | p1 | v2.0 QA remediation (2026-10-01), PROD-040 (release process) | `backend/Containerfile` lines 1-8 (build-info stage: ARG APP_GIT_SHA=unknown, date -u build timestamp, no git installation), line 55 (COPY --from=build-info) | **Problem:** Build-info stage installed git and COPYed entire context to extract SHA via `git rev-parse HEAD`. Since .dockerignore excludes .git/, git rev-parse always failed → "Build: unknown". Added ~50MB to intermediate image for no benefit. **Fix:** Replaced with `ARG APP_GIT_SHA=unknown` passed from host: `podman compose build --build-arg APP_GIT_SHA=$(git rev-parse HEAD)`. **Build evidence:** `podman build --build-arg APP_GIT_SHA=abc1234test` → build-env.sh contains correct SHA and ISO 8601 timestamp. Related: PROD-030, PROD-040. **Commit required** for verified_done. |

Resolver architecture unchanged — only copy was wrong. **Exact-binary validation (2026-10-06):** on the resolved oc-mirror (4.22.0 stream, global `latest`), both `oc-mirror version` and `oc-mirror --v2 version` exit 0, so the guide's bare `oc-mirror version` command was left as-is. | DOC-150 | Operator cross-minor stale-state and catalog leakage (theme V) | done_pending_verification | p1 | v2.0 QA remediation (2026-10-02), authoritative theme map V; root-caused and completed in v2.0 QA-closure pass (2026-10-06), requirement R5 | `frontend/src/shared/operatorMinorReconciliation.js` (new: intent/resolved/active separation, invalidation + post-scan reconciliation), `frontend/src/App.jsx` (`lockAndProceed` now invalidates and flags review; `setActiveStep` review-flag merge made functional), `frontend/src/shared/versionReleaseTransition.js` (shares the invalidation shape), `frontend/src/steps/OperatorsStep.jsx` (pending vs active quick-pick presentation, post-scan reconciliation, unresolved-intent conflict banner), `frontend/src/styles.css` (`.scenario-pick.pending`, light + dark), `frontend/tests/operator-minor-reconciliation.test.js` (36 tests), `frontend/tests/operator-cross-minor-review-state.test.jsx` (7 App-level tests) | **Symptom (human QA 2026-10-05):** after 4.21 -> 4.20 the generated ImageSet was correctly free of the 4.21 catalog, but the Operators step was not marked Needs review and quick picks chosen under 4.21 still rendered as actively selected. **Root cause (proven, three parts):** (1) `confirmChangeRelease` calls `computeReleaseTransition(state, currentMinor)` — target equals current, so the cross-minor branch never fires on unlock; (2) the live re-lock path is `lockAndProceed`, which had no operator invalidation at all, while `confirmReleaseAndProceed` — the only code that set `reviewFlags.operators` — is unreachable dead code (`setShowReleaseWarning(true)` is never called anywhere); (3) `setActiveStep` rebuilt `reviewFlags` from a stale closure and clobbered any flag set immediately before navigating. Generated-output safety came from an independent generator guard (`backend/src/generate.js:1668`, drops operators whose catalog tag != current minor), which is why output looked correct while UI state was stale. **Contract now:** intent (`selected[].name`/`.sources`, `pendingScenarios`) is preserved; resolved metadata (catalogImage, defaultChannel, catalogs, version, scanJobs) is invalidated; active selection (`scenarios`) is cleared so no quick pick reads as current; Operators is flagged for review; after a successful current-minor scan `reconcileOperatorsForMinor` restores valid selections to active and surfaces unavailable packages as an explicit conflict. Generator guard retained. Durable rule recorded in `docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` ("Operator selections across a minor change"). Cross-ref: DOC-077 (operator version mismatch after import), DOC-158. **Commit required** for verified_done. **Display-coherence follow-up (2026-10-06, final bounded pass).** Final manual retest confirmed the stale-output safety work above is correct, but exposed one residual *presentation* incoherence: after reconciling onto the new minor the Quick Pick was active and the operator was present in the generated ImageSet with correct new-minor metadata, yet the **Selected Operators** section appeared empty until the user unselected/reselected. **Root cause (presentation only — canonical state, Quick Pick state and generator input were all already correct):** `OperatorsStep.actualRowCount` was a `useMemo` that read the `selectedGridRef` **ref** while declaring only `[selected.length]` as a dependency. A ref is not reactive and is null on the first render, so the memo returned 0 before the grid node existed and never re-ran unless the selected *count* changed. Reconciliation replaces the selected entries while keeping the count identical (1 -> 1), so the stale 0 survived; combined with the wrapper's inline `maxHeight: Math.min(selectedGridRows, actualRowCount) * 140` and `.selected-grid-wrapper { overflow-y: auto }`, the already-rendered cards were clipped to `max-height: 0px`. Toggling a selection changed the count, re-ran the memo with the ref attached, and the section reappeared — which is exactly why an unselect/reselect cycle 'fixed' it. **Blast radius:** every mount of the Operators step that starts with a non-empty `operators.selected` whose length does not subsequently change — cross-minor reconciliation (reported), run import, and page reload/state restore. **Fix:** measure the grid in a `useLayoutEffect` (ResizeObserver, with a `window.resize` fallback where ResizeObserver is unavailable) into `selectedGridWidth` state, and derive `actualRowCount` from that; floor the row count at 1 whenever anything is selected so an unmeasured grid can never hide operators that are part of the current selection and generated output. No change to `reconcileOperatorsForMinor`, to canonical state, or to the generator — those were proven already coherent. **Durable rule:** the three active-selection representations — Quick Pick styling, the Selected Operators section, and the generated ImageSet — must all derive from the same reconciled canonical state and must never disagree; no view may require an artificial unselect/reselect cycle to resynchronise. Recorded in `docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md`. **Tests:** `frontend/tests/selected-operators-visibility.test.jsx` (15, new — reproduction pinned at `max-height: 0px` before the fix), App-level display-coherence coverage both directions plus an unavailable-package case in `frontend/tests/operator-cross-minor-review-state.test.jsx` (18 total), and `backend/test/operator-cross-minor-imageset-coherence.test.js` (10, new — reconciled operator emitted under the current-minor catalog, unreconciled intent omitted and never fabricated, stale previous-minor tag dropped). **Commit required** for verified_done. |

Without REGISTRY_AUTH_FILE: normal operation. **Root cause:** distribution/distribution v3 maps `REGISTRY_*` env vars to registry config fields via Viper. `REGISTRY_AUTH_FILE` maps to `auth.file` which is not a valid config field, causing panic during local cache registry initialization. **Code is correct:** Backend strips REGISTRY_AUTH_FILE before spawning oc-mirror, passes auth via `--authfile=` flag instead. **README updated:** Wording changed from "may misinterpret" to definitive "causes a goroutine panic at startup." | DOC-155 | Operator catalog tag-vs-digest reference in disk-to-mirror workflows (theme F) | active | p2 | v2.0 QA remediation (2026-10-02), authoritative theme map F | `backend/src/operators.js` (catalog passed to oc-mirror as `--catalog=<image>`), generated ImageSetConfiguration catalog entries | **Concern raised; not reproduced or root-caused.** Operator catalogs referenced by mutable tag rather than immutable digest can make mirror-to-disk and disk-to-mirror runs non-reproducible over time: the same configuration can resolve to different catalog content between the disk capture and the later push, and between environments. **Not yet decided:** whether generated ImageSetConfiguration should pin operator catalogs by digest, and whether the Operators scan should surface the resolved digest to the user. **Scope boundary:** distinct from PROD-030, which owns oc/oc-mirror *binary* pinning (implemented, pending commit), and from DOC-147, which owns Field Guide disk-to-mirror command syntax. **Status: active** — requires a product decision plus evidence before closure. |
| DOC-160 | Generated preview presents a stale document as current after a generation failure | done_pending_verification | p1 | v2.0 QA remediation (2026-10-05), secondary defect surfaced while root-causing [DOC-159]; escalated by the human as a correctness issue in its own right | `frontend/src/App.jsx` (preview effect failure branch invalidates `previewFiles`), `frontend/src/components/YamlDrawer.jsx` (`renderSplitView` error gate; Download disabled on error/loading in `renderSingleConfig` and `renderImageSetWithSwitching`), `frontend/tests/preview-stale-on-failure.test.jsx` (17 tests) | **Distinct from [DOC-159]:** DOC-159 was one *trigger* (an incomplete repeatable row made the generator throw). This is the broader *lifecycle* defect that turned any such throw into a silently wrong answer, and it remains reachable from any future generation or transport failure. Recorded separately on purpose. **Symptom:** after a failed generation for the current state the UI kept rendering the previously generated files as though they described it. **Mechanism:** `setPreviewFiles` ran only in the success branch; the failure branch set `previewError` but left the old files in state. `renderSingleConfig` did gate its *content* on `!loading && !error`, but **`renderSplitView` — the Agent-Based path, which is exactly where the bond-member report came from — had no error or loading gate at all** and kept painting the previous install-config/agent-config. Worse, every pane's Download is a client-side blob built from `previewFiles`, and in the single-config view it stayed **enabled** while the error was displayed, so stale YAML was one click from being saved as current output. **Blast radius:** YAML Preview (both split and single views), the imageset view, and the drawer's per-pane Download. The Assets/Review download is unaffected — it goes to `/api/bundle.zip`, which regenerates from backend state and fails closed on its own. **Fail-closed contract implemented:** a failed generation for the current state invalidates the generated files (`setPreviewFiles({})`), the existing error state is surfaced, and no Download is offered while nothing current exists. Only generated output is dropped — `state`, everything the user typed, is untouched — and the next successful generation repopulates normally. The split view now explains that the previous files are no longer shown rather than silently blanking. Normal field-level validation is deliberately **not** routed through this path: `showIncompleteWarning` still renders a partially generatable document, so an incomplete form is not mistaken for a transport failure. **Request-order race: checked, not assumed — no defect.** Each request takes a monotonic id from `previewRequestIdRef` before it is sent, and **both** the success and failure branches ignore a response whose id is no longer current; the effect cleanup also aborts the in-flight controller, and the abort path returns before invalidating anything. A slow older response therefore cannot overwrite or blank a newer one. No change was needed; the guard is now pinned by tests so it cannot be dropped. **Tests:** `preview-stale-on-failure.test.jsx` 17/17 — success renders; failure stops presenting the previous document and surfaces the error; no Download during failure in the split view and a disabled Download in the single-config view; recovery shows the repaired document and not the previous one; transport failure behaves identically; incomplete-configuration UX is unaffected; invalidation is in the failure branch and not the abort branch; the failure branch touches no user state; and the out-of-order guards are asserted on both branches. **Manual proof required:** drive a generation failure in the real UI and confirm the YAML Preview does not keep showing the prior document, then repair and confirm it repopulates. **Commit required** for verified_done. |
| DOC-161 | Info/help popover clipped outside the usable viewport | done_pending_verification | p1 | v2.0 QA-closure pass (2026-10-06), requirement R1, human screenshot of the Platform Specifics info popover | `frontend/src/shared/popoverPlacement.js` (new pure helper `computePopoverPlacement`), `frontend/src/components/FieldLabelWithInfo.jsx` (measure-then-place, flip/clamp, bounded max-height, reposition on scroll/resize), `frontend/src/styles.css` (`.field-tooltip-portal` column flex; `.field-tooltip-content` max-height moved from CSS constant to JS-computed bound), `frontend/tests/popover-viewport-placement.test.js` (27 tests), `frontend/tests/field-help-popover-viewport.test.jsx` (11 tests) | **Symptom:** with the page scrolled so the trigger sat high, the help popover opened upward past the top of the viewport; the first visible line was mid-content and the earlier text was unreachable. Internal scrolling did not help because the panel's own box started off-screen. **Root cause (proven):** `updatePosition()` chose placement `above` for any trigger with `rect.top >= 120` without ever measuring the panel, set `top = rect.top - gap`, then the style subtracted `gap` a second time and applied `transform: translateY(-100%)`. Rendered top was therefore `rect.top - 16 - panelHeight`, negative whenever the panel was taller than the space above. `max-height` was the CSS constant `min(360px, 70vh)`, unrelated to actual available space. **Fix:** render the panel hidden for one paint to measure its natural size, then compute placement from real geometry: prefer above, flip below, anchor to the roomier side with a bounded height, and clamp against the whole viewport when both sides are too cramped; clamp top/left to a 12px margin; apply the computed max-height so overflow scrolls inside the panel; recompute on resize and (for persistent popovers) on page scroll. **Adjacent latent defect fixed:** the scroll handler called `Node.contains(e.target)` with `window` as the target for page-level scrolls, which throws. Escape/Close/click-outside/focus behaviour preserved. Durable rule recorded in `docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` ("Help and Info Popovers"). **Commit required** for verified_done. |
