No change was needed there; genericity is now proven by tests driven with a hypothetical later minor. **Defect found:** five hint/subtitle strings hardcoded provenance as `"Introduced in OpenShift " + selectedMinor` (AWS confidentialCompute, AWS rootVolume.throughput, Azure allowSharedKeyAccess, and two bmcVerifyCA sites). Those report *provenance*, which does not change as the user re-targets, so under a later locked target they would have misstated history. They now read the field's real `introducedInMinor` from catalog metadata. **No 4.22 support enabled:** `SUPPORTED_MINORS` remains `["4.20", "4.21"]`; `isSupportedMinor("4.22")` is still false; `getCatalogForScenario(..., "4.22")` still throws fail-closed — all asserted in the new test file. The hypothetical minor used to prove genericity (4.99) is synthetic catalog data only. Durable rule recorded in `docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` ("Introduced-version annotations: provenance vs contextual badge"). **Commit required** for verified_done. |
| DOC-163 | AWS root-volume throughput did not update the live YAML preview until blur | done_pending_verification | p3 | v2.0 QA-closure pass (2026-10-06), requirement R3, human 4.21 smoke | `frontend/src/steps/PlatformSpecificsStep.jsx` (new `commitAwsRootVolumeThroughput` helper; input commits on change as well as blur), `frontend/tests/aws-throughput-live-preview.test.jsx` (9 tests) | **Symptom:** replica spinners updated the live preview immediately, but root-volume throughput only reached the preview after focus left the input. Final generated output was always correct, so this was a consistency defect, not a data-correctness defect. **Root cause:** the field follows the step's established local-draft pattern (`localAwsRootVolumeThroughput`) and committed to canonical state only in `onBlur`. **Scope decision:** fixed narrowly for this one field rather than converting the ~60 sibling draft fields — that would be a form-architecture rewrite and is explicitly out of scope. Valid edits now commit as typed; invalid intermediate text (e.g. `1` on the way to `125`) is held in local state only, leaving canonical state at its last good value, and the range error is still surfaced on blur so the accepted first-entry/touch validation behaviour is unchanged. **Remaining:** other AWS numeric fields (rootVolumeSize, rootVolumeIops) and the wider draft-field population still commit on blur — see findings queue. **Commit required** for verified_done. |
| DOC-164 | Zero-failure release gate: backend production audit and three non-deterministic frontend tests | done_pending_verification | p0 | v2.0 zero-failure release-gate cleanup (2026-10-06); human policy decision that "pre-existing" is provenance, not a waiver | `backend/package-lock.json` (transitive refresh only; `backend/package.json` untouched), `frontend/tests/aws-ami-auto-populate.test.jsx`, `frontend/tests/preview-no-platform-gate.test.jsx`, `frontend/tests/import-reload-override.test.jsx`, `frontend/tests/operator-cross-minor-review-state.test.jsx` | **A. Backend production audit (GHSA-jqcg-44mw-7w3h, CVSS 9.1, CWE-290/348/697).** `proxy-addr` 2.0.7 (vulnerable `>=1.1.0 <2.0.8`) reached the production graph transitively through `express@4.22.3`. Express declares `proxy-addr: ~2.0.7`, which already admits the fixed 2.0.8, so the correct resolution was an in-contract lockfile refresh (`npm update proxy-addr`) — **no** override, no `audit-level` suppression, no Express version change, and `package.json` unmodified. 2.0.8 has an identical transitive graph (forwarded 0.2.0, ipaddr.js 1.9.1). Installed `node_modules` had also drifted to express 4.22.2 against a lockfile pinning 4.22.3; `npm ci` restored agreement. A dev-only moderate (`fast-copy` 4.0.3 via `pino-pretty`, GHSA-jggr-w7fw-pc2j) was resolved the same in-contract way (`^4.0.0` admits 4.1.2). Result: `npm audit --omit=dev` **and** full `npm audit` both report 0 vulnerabilities, exit 0; backend 1814 pass / 0 fail; `npm ci` reproducible; better-sqlite3 native module loads; live server smoke serves `/api/health`, `/api/state`, `/api/build-info`, `/api/operators/credentials` and the `X-Forwarded-For` path (proxy-addr's actual surface). **B. Three non-deterministic frontend tests — one shared root cause.** All three clicked or changed a control that was still `disabled`, or read the DOM before the element existed; such interactions are **silent no-ops** in jsdom, so the subsequent `waitFor` timed out on a consequence that could never happen. (1) `aws-ami-auto-populate` waited for the Refresh button's *presence* while the mount-time auto-lookup still had it disabled — proven deterministically by injecting a 120 ms lookup delay. (2) `preview-no-platform-gate` "version transition 4.21 -> 4.20" never performed a transition at all: `rerender(<App />)` cannot change store state, so it passed only when its 50 ms poll happened to land between two start-up generate calls ~9 ms apart; it was rewritten to drive the real unlock -> channel -> re-lock flow and assert the **content** of `POST /api/generate` rather than a count delta. (3) `import-reload-override` read `input[type=file]` synchronously after waiting for "Loading" to vanish — text that may never render — so it queried before the wizard existed. `operator-cross-minor-review-state` was additionally hardened: it waited for `operators` to be *defined*, which is true of the initial state too, so it raced the debounced persist; it now waits on `operators.version === null`, the marker only the invalidation sets. **No timeout was increased anywhere** — every fix synchronises on the causal state. **Evidence:** per-file stress 25/25, 30/30, 30/30, 25/25; full frontend suite **15/15 consecutive green** (2776 passed / 2 skipped). Baseline for comparison, measured on an unmodified `git archive` of HEAD e8d6714: AMI test failed ~1/6 under load and `preview-no-platform-gate` failed 1/12 in isolation. **No product code was changed for B** — product behaviour was verified identical in both trees (same two generate calls, same timing). **Commit required** for verified_done. |
| DOC-159 | Incomplete repeatable rows abort artifact generation and freeze the YAML Preview on a stale document | done_pending_verification | p1 | v2.0 QA remediation (2026-10-05), human QA: "newly added Bond Members appear in the node editor UI but do NOT appear in the live agent-config.yaml YAML Preview" | `backend/src/generate.js` (`addBondMemberEthernets` helper, four primary/additional bond call sites; `hasResolvableIdentity` guard on the additional-interface loop), `backend/test/repeatable-collection-generation.test.js` (16 tests) | **Symptom:** A bond member added in the node editor rendered in the UI but never appeared in the live agent-config.yaml preview; only the original members did. Removing a member behaved likewise. **The human's framing was that UI state did not contain the new member. That was disproven first:** `updateNode` in `HostInventoryV2Step.jsx` handles function patches correctly and writes through `updateInventory` to canonical `state.hostInventory`, and the preview effect in `App.jsx` depends on `state?.hostInventory`, so a new object reference does re-trigger generation. The generator is also **not** cardinality-limited — three fully populated members always generated correctly (proven by direct `buildAgentConfig` reproduction before any edit). **Immediate mechanism:** a just-added member is `{ name: "", macAddress: "" }`. Its empty name reached `addEthernet`, and the interface-name invariant at the end of `buildNmState` threw `NMState interface name must not be empty.` **Lifecycle cause:** that throw failed the whole `POST /api/generate` request. `setPreviewFiles` only runs on success, so the previously rendered document stayed on screen — the UI showed a stale two-member YAML rather than an error. The member was never "dropped"; generation never completed. **Blast radius:** every artifact produced by that request — live YAML Preview, the Assets/review preview and the downloadable agent-config.yaml — since `/api/generate` (preview) and `/api/bundle.zip` (download) both call the same `buildAgentConfig(state)`. Any incomplete row reaching NMState had the same effect, not bond members alone. **Fix:** incomplete repeatable rows are skipped during generation, matching the rule `extraRoutes` already used (entries missing a destination or next hop are ignored). `addBondMemberEthernets` skips unnamed bond members at all four primary/additional call sites; `hasResolvableIdentity` skips an additional interface whose identifying name is blank. Validation still reports both to the user ("Bond member interface name is required.", "Additional ethernet interface name is required."). A **genuine** duplicate name — two interfaces the user actually named the same thing — still throws; skipping incomplete rows was not allowed to become tolerating conflicts. **Audit (requested, broader than the reported field):** bond members (primary + additional), additional ethernet/VLAN interfaces, static routes, NTP servers and vSphere failure domains were each exercised for add / edit / remove against the real generators. Two defects found, both the same cause and both fixed above; routes, NTP and vSphere failure domains were already correct (a blank failure domain is cleanly filtered, not emitted malformed). **Secondary defect, now owned by [DOC-160]:** on any generation failure the UI retained the last successful `previewFiles` instead of surfacing the error, which is what turned a backend throw into a silent wrong answer. DOC-159 removed this trigger; the generic stale-on-error lifecycle defect was fixed separately under DOC-160 (fail-closed invalidation + error surfacing + no stale download). The two are tracked apart on purpose: DOC-159 is a trigger, DOC-160 is the lifecycle defect. **UI-path evidence completed (2026-10-05):** `frontend/tests/repeatable-collections-ui-path.test.jsx` (9 tests) closes the other half of the chain for Additional Interfaces and static Routes — real editor controls to canonical state for add, edit, order and remove, plus proof that a cleared interface name stays in state for validation to report and that presentation mode does not alter bond-member data. Combined with the backend generator tests and the live API runs above, UI to state to generated output is proven for both collections. **Tests:** `repeatable-collection-generation.test.js` 16/16 — two members generate two; add a third propagates; a blank just-added member does not throw; it appears once named; remove drops it everywhere with no hole, duplicate or reorder; add/remove/add stays deterministic; edits propagate; generation is pure across repeated calls; preview and bundle provably share the generator; vlan-on-bond, additional-interface bonds, cleared additional-interface names, routes and vSphere failure domains all covered; duplicate names still rejected. Backend suite 1806 pass / 0 fail / 5 todo. **Manual proof required:** real browser path — add a third member, confirm it reaches YAML Preview, Assets preview and the downloaded agent-config.yaml, then remove one and confirm it disappears from all three. **Commit required** for verified_done. |

Previously `displayName` was rendered last as a trailing subtle line. **Fallback:** when `displayName` is absent/blank the package name becomes the heading and the `Package:` line is omitted, so no card ever shows the same string twice. **Canonical identity deliberately unchanged:** `op.name` remains the package identifier and `op.id` the selection key; `selectOperator`/`removeOperator` still key on `op.id`; generated ImageSetConfiguration still emits `name: op.name` (`backend/src/generate.js`, which never references `displayName`) — existing coverage at `backend/test/operator-version-constraints.test.js:45`. **Search:** no operator search/filter input exists in `OperatorsStep.jsx` today (`filteredCatalogs` only excludes already-selected entries), so there was nothing to preserve or extend; a displayName-aware search would be new scope. **Tests:** 11/11 — hierarchy and ordering at both sites, fallback with no duplicate Package line, identity keyed on id not displayName, and generator free of displayName. | DOC-157 | Tool-acquisition defects: unsupported tar flag and v2-incompatible oc-mirror preflight | done_pending_verification | p0 | v2.0 QA remediation (2026-10-02), human report of `tools/oc-mirror.ERROR.txt`, requirement D3 | `backend/src/ocMirrorRuntime.js` (`extractMember` tar invocation; `runPreflight` now `--v2 version`; `supportsV2ListOperators`), `backend/test/binary-integrity.test.js` | **Symptom:** a downloadable asset bundle with "Include tools" enabled contained `tools/oc-mirror.ERROR.txt` reading `Failed to include oc/oc-mirror: Could not fetch oc/oc-mirror for architecture x86_64.` **Reproduced** on the current branch through the real HTTP path (`POST /api/bundle.zip`, target 4.21, arch x86_64) against the isolated checklist stack — not via a helper call. **Root cause 1 (proven):** `extractOc`/`extractOcMirror` passed `--no-absolute-filenames`, which is a cpio option. GNU tar 1.34 — shipped in BOTH `ubi9/nodejs-20` and `ubi9/nodejs-22` — rejects it with `tar: unrecognized option`. Every download-based extraction therefore threw; the caller's candidate loop swallowed the exception and surfaced only the generic "Could not fetch" message. Verified by replaying the loop as UID 1001 inside the container. **Not a Node 20→22 regression:** both base images were checked and neither supports the flag. The defect predates this branch and was masked because the baked-in-binary shortcut usually satisfied export requests without extracting; it became load-bearing once tool acquisition moved to dynamic resolution. **Root cause 2 (build-specific, re-validated 2026-10-06):** `runPreflight` executed a bare `oc-mirror version`. On the oc-mirror build observed at the time this item was raised, that exits non-zero with `the use of the flag --v1 or --v2 is mandatory`, so the baked-in tier could never pass preflight. **Correction:** this is NOT universal to oc-mirror v2. Re-validated against the binary the global-latest policy actually resolves today (`4.22.0-202609221258.p2.g5b31abd.assembly.stream.el9-5b31abd` at `/usr/local/bin/oc-mirror`): both `oc-mirror version` and `oc-mirror --v2 version` exit 0 with identical output. The original wording overstated the claim. `runPreflight` keeps `--v2 version` because it is correct on **both** behaviours; no product change was made to satisfy this correction. **Durable requirement:** invocation syntax must be valid for the exact resolved binary and verified against that binary, never against a remembered or assumed vendor contract — oc-mirror behaviour varies across builds, and the global-latest oc-mirror policy means the resolved build legitimately changes over time (see CLAUDE.md "External tool version policy"). **Fixes:** drop the bogus tar flag (GNU tar already strips leading `/` unless `-P`); preflight with `--v2 version`; add `supportsV2ListOperators()` capability gate; surface the real acquisition error instead of a generic message. **Cross-ref:** DOC-128 (v2 listing), PROD-030 (tool policy). **Commit required** for verified_done. |
| DOC-156 | Minor-release onboarding: revalidate Target Platform × Installation Method × CPU Architecture matrix (theme R7) | active | p2 | v2.0 QA remediation (2026-10-02), authoritative requirement R7 | `frontend/src/steps/` Blueprint CPU Architecture enable/disable rules (currently derived from a 4.20-era support scan) | **NOT a v2.0 deliverable — do not implement in v2.0.** Scheduled for the next minor-release onboarding effort, immediately before/within OCP 4.22 onboarding. **Requirement:** every minor-release onboarding must revalidate the supported Target Platform × Installation Method × CPU Architecture combinations and update Blueprint CPU-Architecture enable/disable behavior accordingly. Current UI behavior originated from a 4.20-era scan and must not be assumed to hold forever. **Authority model — PRIMARY:** the version-specific official Red Hat OpenShift Installation Overview HTML tables for that minor. **SECONDARY cross-check:** release-tagged `openshift/installer` platform/architecture validation rules for the same minor. Avoid PDF scraping where versioned HTML exists; do not assume `openshift-install` emits the full matrix unless proven. **Acceptance:** acquire the authoritative matrix for the new minor → diff against the previous supported minor → emit a machine-readable `platformArchitectureMatrix` → human/agent review → update Blueprint enable/disable rules → generate parameterized tests for every platform × method × architecture combination → record source/provenance/version → **fail onboarding if the authority is ambiguous** rather than silently carrying prior-minor assumptions forward. **Annotation (v2.1, Tranche 2, 2026-10-07):** the machine-readable matrix now exists as `data/arch-support/{4.20,4.21,4.22}.json` in the D3 shape — minor x platform x install-method x architecture, 144 cells, every cell carrying a disposition, an explicit reason and per-cell provenance that resolves to a declared same-minor source. Guarded by `scripts/validate-arch-support.js` (registered in `scripts/validate-param-authority.js`) with 19 hermetic tests. Each minor was placed from its OWN books, read separately; they agree, which is a finding rather than an assumption. **The data contradicts the shipped table and that contradiction is the point of DOC-156:** `BlueprintStep.jsx` `PLATFORM_ARCH_SUPPORT` claims vSphere aarch64, AWS GovCloud aarch64 and bare-metal ppc64le/s390x, none of which the 4.20, 4.21 or 4.22 platform books support — each book documents `amd64 (the default)` for vSphere, Nutanix and IBM Cloud, and `amd64 and arm64` for bare metal, AWS and Azure. Every cell is resolved: **zero `unknown`** remain. The 7 previously open cells per minor were closed from same-minor evidence — bare-metal/agent `ppc64le`/`s390x` are IBM Power / IBM Z installations the agent book handles through dedicated z/VM, RHEL KVM and LPAR procedures for separate platforms Architect does not model; vSphere/agent `aarch64` is governed by the vSphere book's own `amd64 (the default)` statement, which the installer-wide agent table does not override; and AWS GovCloud / Azure Government `aarch64` is unaddressed by every government-region section at all three minors, which under the documentation-veto rule is not-offered rather than supported. All are `hidden` and FAIL CLOSED. The `unknown` disposition is retained in the schema so a future onboarding can record an in-flight gap, and `scripts/validate-arch-support.test.js` asserts the tracked data ships none. Each file also carries `homogeneousOnly: true` with the verbatim same-minor citation *"Currently, clusters with varied architectures are not supported. All pools must specify the same architecture."*, and keeps the three architecture axes separate including the standing rule that the cluster-node FIPS statement is NOT an export-binary restriction. **Annotation (v2.1, Tranche 3, 2026-10-08): runtime migration COMPLETE.** `data/arch-support/**` is now the runtime authority. `BlueprintStep.jsx` `PLATFORM_ARCH_SUPPORT` is DELETED and replaced by `shared/archSupport.js` (pure, dataset-injected, fails closed on unknown minor/scenario/architecture/malformed version with no cross-minor fallback) behind `frontend/src/archSupportResolver.js`, which gates loading on `SUPPORTED_MINORS`. A generated UI projection (`frontend/src/data/arch-support/<minor>.json`, via `scripts/sync-arch-support.js --check`) carries disposition/offered/summary only; the 137 KB of audit `reason` and `provenance` prose stays canonical-only, so the eager bundle grew 1,150 KB -> 1,191 KB against a 1,320 KB limit rather than ~92 KB more. Each canonical cell gained an additive one-sentence `summary` (144 insertions, nothing else changed). Coverage: 321 tests in `frontend/tests/arch-support-resolver.test.jsx`, **288 of them parameterized over every one of the 144 cells** (canonical enforcement plus projection fidelity), and 9 rendered-behaviour tests in `frontend/tests/blueprint-architecture-d3.test.jsx`. Three of those rendered assertions fail against the old constant: vSphere `aarch64` and AWS GovCloud `aarch64` are now correctly closed, and an unrecognised platform no longer falls through `|| archOptions.map(...)` to enable all four architectures. The disabled tooltip now names the OpenShift minor and the documentation instead of `Not supported on <platform>`. The three architecture axes remain separated, and `frontend/tests/fips-installer-architectures.test.jsx` is untouched. **Remaining:** DOC-156 stays `active` only for the per-combination *generation* tests over real install-config output; the resolver and its data are done. **Post-flip certification complete (Tranche 6).** The real-output residual is now closed at every supported minor: 144 D3 cells (4.20, 4.21 and 4.22 at 15 supported / 33 hidden each) verified against real parsed install-config and imageset output, 0 failures, 0 locked cells (vacuous, and a test fails if one is ever added). The G2 guard is re-proven closed after the flip on the route it was really about — a crafted `POST /api/generate` — as well as through the builders and a stale imported state; a supported 4.22 cell on the same route is accepted and emits `architecture: arm64`, so the guard is not passing vacuously. Evidence: `docs/minor-release/4.22/TRANCHE_6_POST_FLIP_CERTIFICATION.md` §3. |
| DOC-165 | Complete generated-config parameter coverage, starting with agent-config.yaml NMState networkConfig | active | p1 | Tranche 0B closure (2026-10-07); `docs/minor-release/CATALOG_REPAIR_LEDGER_0B.md`; `docs/PARAM_AUTHORITY.md` | Gap example in canonical data: `data/params/4.20/bare-metal-agent.json` and `data/params/4.21/bare-metal-agent.json` model `hosts[].networkConfig.interfaces[].link-aggregation`, `.link-aggregation.port` and `.link-aggregation.mode`, but `link-aggregation.options` is only described in prose on the parent object; no catalog row exists for any individual option | **NOT a Tranche 0B deliverable — not implemented in 0B.** **Goal:** for every structured configuration artifact Airgap Architect generates, every Red Hat-supported field, value and combination for an in-scope scenario and supported OCP minor has an explicit application disposition and complete coverage, or an explicit documented support boundary. **Immediate priority:** `agent-config.yaml` NMState `networkConfig`. **Motivating example:** audit every Red Hat-supported `link-aggregation.options.*` field, including as applicable `miimon`, `primary`, `lacp_rate`, `min_links`, `xmit_hash_policy`, plus every other option established by authoritative same-minor research. **Do NOT equate upstream NMState capability with Red Hat OpenShift support** — docs hold veto authority over user-facing supportedness. **Data rules:** canonical supported-parameter metadata goes in `data/params/<minor>/<scenario>.json` for each applicable minor and scenario, with explicit nested paths such as `hosts[].networkConfig.interfaces[].link-aggregation.options.miimon` rather than prose on the parent; each row carries the normal authoritative metadata (outputFile, type, allowed, default, requiredness, applies_to, supportStatus/minVersion/maxVersion, conditional and mode constraints, exact citations). `data/docs-index/<minor>.json` gains a row only where the authoritative source document belongs to that minor and scenario's documentation set — never one row per parameter duplicating a catalog citation; parameter-level citations stay in `data/params`. `frontend/src/data/catalogs/<minor>/**` and `frontend/src/data/docs-index/<minor>.json` remain generated mirrors and are never hand-edited. **Research scope, minimum:** interface base fields, Ethernet, SR-IOV where applicable, IPv4, IPv6, MTU, bond and link-aggregation, all Red Hat-supported bond modes, every supported `link-aggregation.options.*` field, mode-specific constraints, VLAN, DNS resolver, routes, route tables and rules where supported, and any further Host Network Management / NMState subtree supported by the Agent-based workflow. **Required coverage matrix per minor and path:** parameter path, authoritative source, allowed values, conditional and mode constraints, catalog disposition, frontend control, frontend validation, backend validation, backend generation, import/export round-trip, preview, automated tests, Field Guide help, gap and action. **Success criterion:** zero Red Hat-supported in-scope generated configuration values lack an explicit frontend and backend disposition. **Then extend the same audit model** to `install-config.yaml`, `imageset-config.yaml` and every other structured generated artifact. **Reconciles rather than duplicates** PHX-007, PHX-025 and PHX-034, which are narrower slices of this same surface and are annotated to point here. |
| DOC-166 | Single global oc-mirror-v2 / ImageSetConfiguration structural authority, decoupled from target OCP minor | active | p1 | Tranche 0B finding F-0B-1 (reframed); `CLAUDE.md` "External tool version policy"; `docs/minor-release/CATALOG_REPAIR_LEDGER_0B.md` | `data/params/4.20/oc-mirror-v2.json` exists; `data/params/4.21/` has no counterpart, and `data/docs-index/4.21.json` lists no such scenario, so no gate fires | **NOT a Tranche 0B deliverable — not implemented in 0B.** **Supersedes the naive reading of F-0B-1.** The fix is explicitly **NOT** to create per-minor oc-mirror-v2 catalogs for parity: product policy is that oc-mirror v2 is the latest supported official v2 **globally**, independent of the target OCP minor, so `data/params/4.21/oc-mirror-v2.json` and `data/params/4.22/oc-mirror-v2.json` must **not** be produced by copying the 4.20 file. **Required work:** investigate and re-home the authority model so generic ImageSetConfiguration and oc-mirror-v2 structure and validation have ONE canonical globally scoped authority, provenance-bound to the exact latest oc-mirror v2 release, documentation and binary that were validated. **Separate two concerns.** GLOBAL oc-mirror-v2 structural capability: ImageSetConfiguration schema, field types, requiredness, oc-mirror-specific validation, generic options and features. TARGET-MINOR values: platform channel and release content, OCP release versions, operator index and catalog tags, and anything else genuinely determined by the selected minor. **Investigate the correct canonical output-specific location first** rather than assuming `data/params/<minor>/`. **Migration acceptance:** generated behavior preserved, plus tests proving every supported target minor consumes the same structural oc-mirror authority while emitting correct target-minor-specific values. | **Annotation (v2.1, Tranche 3, 2026-10-08):** audited. The substantive migration landed in Tranche 1.5 — the global authority exists at `data/oc-mirror-v2/imageset-config-schema.json`, `buildImageSetConfig()` selects no schema truth by target minor, and **no production module reads `data/params/<minor>/oc-mirror-v2.json`** (verified across every non-test file in `backend/src`, `frontend/src`, `shared`). The frontend performs no ImageSetConfig validation, so there is no second authority to disagree. Tranche 3 adds `backend/test/oc-mirror-authority-location.test.js` (10 tests), which fails if an `oc-mirror-v2.json` is cloned into any minor, if 4.22 gains one, if a production module starts reading the legacy file, if a schema path is built from a minor, or if the generation gate stops rejecting unsupported minors. **The legacy `data/params/4.20/oc-mirror-v2.json` is RETAINED**: inert at runtime, read only by tooling that walks `data/params/**` wholesale, and retiring it would change the 4.20 catalog count 13 -> 12 and drop a frontend mirror for no runtime benefit. The relocation obligation stays open here; it is not blocking.

Optional configuration parameters` | **NOT a Tranche 0B deliverable — no link cleanup was performed.** **Reader-navigation quality, not an authority failure:** every one of these citations is correct about which book and section is authoritative, and the strict provenance guard passes on all of them. The defect is that clicking one lands at the beginning of the full IBM Cloud book rather than the relevant chapter, unlike the other eleven scenarios whose citations land on the chapter page. **Required work:** for each distinct citation class, verify either the precise same-minor paginated chapter URL or a proven `html-single` fragment anchor, and normalize **only** where the replacement is proven. **Explicitly out of scope:** a blanket `html` to `html-single` migration. That would degrade roughly 714 citations per minor from chapter-targeted to book-root, and doing it without degradation would require verifying an anchor for each — a mass transformation without per-row evidence. **Keep `html-single` as an acquisition and scraping technique** where it improves reliable evidence retrieval (it is already recorded as such in the onboarding runbook, including the caveat that the first ~100 KB of such a page is site-wide navigation rather than the book's own contents). Parameter and catalog citations should continue to prefer the most precise human-usable authoritative URL. If the product ever wants single-page links for an airgapped audience, that belongs in the UI as a render-time transform, not baked into the provenance data. |

The string appears in **no** Red Hat documentation book at either 4.20 or 4.21 | **NOT a Tranche 0B deliverable — no `minVersion` or `supportStatus` was changed on this evidence.** **How it was found:** the field was absent from the April `release-4.20` branch tip Tranche 0B first derived from, and was backported into the 4.20 z-stream afterwards. Exact-release certification against 4.20.40 falsified the recorded "absent at 4.20" claim; the annotation in `scripts/minor/repair/proven-repairs.js` was corrected, and that is the only change made. **Two questions remain, both requiring documentation and therefore a product decision.** (1) **4.20 coverage:** released 4.20 accepts the field, so a 4.20 user may legitimately want it — but installer presence alone does not authorize exposing it (runbook Rule 2.4), and adding a supported parameter to 4.20 is product-scope expansion. (2) **4.21 supportedness:** the field is already `supported-ui` at 4.21 on installer evidence alone, with no Red Hat documentation at that minor either. That is the same class of gap as [DOC-168]. **Required work:** establish same-minor documentation for 4.20 and for 4.21, then decide coverage and `supportStatus` together and consistently across both minors. **Do not resolve one minor in isolation.** |

| DOC-172 | Gitleaks configuration loaded zero rules; all local secret scanning was vacuous | verified_done | p0 | Tranche 1.5 security gate (2026-10-07) | `.gitleaks.toml` (`[extend] useDefault = true` added), `scripts/security/gitleaks-selftest.sh` | **Confirmed defect, now fixed.** `.gitleaks.toml` declared an allowlist but no `[extend] useDefault`, so gitleaks loaded **no rules at all**. Proven by scanning runtime-generated synthetic secrets: a GitHub PAT, an RSA private key, a Slack bot token and two generic API keys were **all missed** under the repository config and **all detected** under default rules. `scripts/check-secrets.sh` and the `check-secrets` pre-commit hook were therefore passing vacuously, and CI's `gitleaks-action` reads the same file. A self-test now runs first inside the tranche gate and fails if the vacuum returns. **Consequence:** findings that were previously invisible are now surfaced and require triage — see DOC-174. |
| DOC-173 | Browser localStorage persisted platform credentials the backend deliberately refused to store | verified_done | p0 | Tranche 1.5 security gate runtime credential canaries (2026-10-07) | `shared/stateSanitizer.js` (new shared single source), `backend/src/stateSanitizer.js` (thin re-export), `frontend/src/store.jsx` `getStateForPersistence`, `frontend/tests/credential-canary-browser-storage.test.js` | **Confirmed defect, now fixed.** `getStateForPersistence` stripped only the pull secrets and vSphere credentials, while the backend's `sanitizeStateForPersistence` also stripped AWS `secretAccessKey`, Azure `clientSecret`, IBM Cloud `apiKey`, Nutanix `prismPassword`, BMC credentials, embedded proxy passwords and `sshPrivateKey`. Those therefore reached browser `localStorage` even though the server refused to persist them — the two boundaries had drifted because the list was duplicated. **Fix:** the sanitizer moved to `shared/stateSanitizer.js` and both boundaries now use one list; the backend path is a re-export so no existing import changed. Nothing functional is lost: the app hydrates from `GET /api/state`, which already returns server-sanitized state. Full frontend suite green after the change. |
| DOC-174 | Triage gitleaks findings newly visible after the rule-loading fix | verified_done | p1 | Tranche 1.5 security forensics (2026-10-07); consequence of DOC-172 | `.gitleaks.toml` exact-value stopwords; `scripts/security/gitleaks-selftest.sh` controls G and H | **Resolved.** All 9 `generic-api-key` findings were individually triaged and proven non-secret: five base64 placeholders decoding to `username:password`, `test:test`, `mirror:test`, `example:secret` and the OKD `id:pass` dummy; a literal e2e test password; a **truncated** JWT prefix in `docs/API.md` ending in `...`; and one pure **source identifier** (`nutanixApiVIPV6`) that the rule matched as a symbol name. Each is allowlisted by EXACT VALUE (stopword) — the narrowest mechanism available — with no path, directory, fixture tree or detector class exempted. Two negative controls prove the scoping: a real synthetic secret planted in a stopword-bearing file still fires (G), and a near-miss of a stopword still fires (H). Worktree findings reduced from 12 to 3; the remainder were covered by the history scan baseline and the current tree is now clean.|

The disposition allowlist in `t4-quick-pick-catalog-verification.test.js` is now **empty**, so the "every package resolves" invariant has no exemptions. | DOC-188 | Advanced NMState bond-option parity (`xmit_hash_policy`, `miimon`, `lacp_rate`, `updelay`, `downdelay`, `primary`, `min_links`) | active | p2 | v2.1 Tranche 7A advanced bond-options parity audit (2026-10-08); evidence `docs/minor-release/4.22/ADVANCED_BOND_OPTIONS_PARITY_AUDIT.md` | `data/params/{4.20,4.21,4.22}/{bare-metal-agent,vsphere-agent}.json`; `frontend/src/data/catalogs/**` mirrors; `frontend/src/components/NodeDrawerAgentContent.jsx`; `frontend/src/steps/HostInventoryV2Step.jsx`; `frontend/src/hostInventoryV2Helpers.js` (`bond: {name, mode, slaves[]}`); `backend/src/generate.js` `addBond()`; `backend/test/nic-bond-vlan-ipv6.test.js` | **NOT release-blocking for v2.1.0 — audited and explicitly scoped.** Shipped today: bond `mode` (UI dropdown, 2 of 7 modes — exactly the two Red Hat documents at 4.22) and bond members (`link-aggregation.port`, from `bond.slaves[]`). **Everything under `link-aggregation.options.*` is absent**: no catalog row at ANY minor, no UI control, no state field, no validation, no test. **`xmit_hash_policy` appears nowhere in production code** — it is not claimed and not silently dropped, it is simply not offered. **Cross-minor parity IS intact:** 4.20, 4.21 and 4.22 carry byte-identical bond rows (`link-aggregation`, `.mode`, `.port`, all `supported-backend-only`) in both agent scenarios, and the frontend mirrors match the canonical catalogs exactly — the 4.22 onboarding neither introduced nor widened this gap. **Why not blocking:** every possible user-facing claim was checked and none exists — no control, no tooltip, no catalog row marked `supported-ui`, no validation, and no documentation telling users to hand-edit state. **Latent trap recorded:** `addBond()` hardcodes `options: { miimon: "100" }` unconditionally and **silently discards any `bond.options` present in state** (reproduced at 4.22: supplied `miimon`/`xmit_hash_policy`/`lacp_rate`/`updelay`/`downdelay` were all replaced by the hardcode). Unreachable through any supported workflow today because no UI path creates that state, but it is the exact line that must become state-driven when this item is implemented — otherwise the UI would accept input the generator drops, which WOULD be release-blocking. No test asserts the hardcoded value. **Blocked on a product decision inherited from DOC-165:** Red Hat documents these NMState fields only by example while upstream NMState supports many more — Option A (documented-by-example only, which would leave `xmit_hash_policy` correctly unexposed), Option B (upstream schema, Red Hat-annotated), or Option C (curated subset + validated raw-NMState escape hatch). The committed research recommends **A for v2.1, C as the direction**; this is a human decision, not an agent decision. **Recommended release target: v2.1.x**, not v2.1.0 — nothing is falsely claimed, there is no regression, bond mode and members (what determines whether a bond comes up at all) are shipped and tested, and implementing before the policy decision would hard-code a support policy by accident. **Acceptance criteria** (12 items, including catalog rows at all three minors, removal of the `addBond()` hardcode with a test proving user input reaches the YAML, mode-specific validation, import/export round-trip, and correcting the `link-aggregation.mode` description whose examples cite `balance-rr` — undocumented at 4.22 — while omitting `802.3ad`, which the UI does offer) are enumerated in the audit §8. **Reconciles with** DOC-165 (the umbrella; this is its narrowly-scoped bond slice), PHX-007 (E2E/UI path coverage), PHX-025 (bond mode naming) and PHX-034 (backend generation test breadth) — none of which establishes `xmit_hash_policy` closure. **RE-SCOPED BY TRANCHE 7A.1 (2026-10-08) — the Tranche 7A conclusion above stands as written but was deliberately narrow.** 7A audited bond options only and recommended keeping them unexposed; the human has since clarified that OAA must have an explicit accounting for the whole relevant Day-1 NMState surface and that useful supported advanced options should be considered for implementation. DOC-188 is therefore now the **advanced-bond child** of umbrella item **DOC-191**, not a standalone decision. **New evidence:** OpenShift 4.22 *Kubernetes NMState* §1.9.4 states verbatim that OpenShift supports exactly `active-backup`, `balance-xor` and `802.3ad` and that "Other bond modes are not supported" — so **`balance-xor` is a supported mode OAA does not offer** (backlog-worthy, not blocking: the dropdown is a subset of supported, never a superset, and nothing claims completeness). The upstream NMState YAML API documents **28** `link-aggregation.options` keys, all now accounted for in the coverage ledger. `options.primary` is the **strongest `supported-ui` candidate** because it is the one bond option Red Hat documents by Day-1 example (§1.9); `xmit_hash_policy`, `lacp_rate` and `min_links` are verified **absent** from the 4.22 Agent book and remain upstream-only. **`miimon` disposition sharpened:** the hardcoded `"100"` has no recorded rationale (present since the initial public release), is documented nowhere in OAA, is mode-agnostic and therefore safe for both offered modes, and is asserted by **no test**. Recommended end state is a user-editable field defaulting to 100 with the default visible in help — NOT omission, since disabling link monitoring by default would be a functional regression. **Mode-gating is now a hard requirement** of any implementation: `lacp_rate`/`min_links` are 802.3ad-only, `xmit_hash_policy` applies to balance-xor/802.3ad only, `primary` is active-backup-only, and `updelay`/`downdelay` are invalid without `miimon`. Per-option matrix with types, values, defaults, companion requirements and switch-side dependencies is in `docs/minor-release/4.22/NMSTATE_NETWORKCONFIG_COVERAGE_AUDIT.md` §5.3. **Still NOT release-blocking** and still **v2.1.x**. |

| DOC-191 | Comprehensive version-aware Day-1 NMState/networkConfig coverage and onboarding automation | active | p1 | v2.1 Tranche 7A.1 NMState coverage audit (2026-10-08); evidence `docs/minor-release/4.22/NMSTATE_NETWORKCONFIG_COVERAGE_AUDIT.md` | `backend/src/generate.js` (`buildNmState()` for agent-config, and the separate inline bare-metal IPI builder ~line 614); `data/params/{4.20,4.21,4.22}/**`; frontend catalog mirrors; node-drawer and Host Inventory UI; `frontend/src/hostInventoryV2Helpers.js`; validation; import/export; `backend/test/nic-bond-vlan-ipv6.test.js`; Field Guide v4.20–v4.22 | **UMBRELLA for the whole Day-1 NMState surface. Supersedes the narrow framing of DOC-188 and complements DOC-165.** Establishes the baseline the human asked for: **every relevant NMState field/family has an explicit disposition and none is accidentally unknown**. **Baseline measured:** 64 paths/families accounted for; OAA emits 26 leaf paths across 8 families (interface core, IPv4, IPv6, bond, VLAN, VRF, routes, DNS); **25 of 26 are schema-valid, 1 is not** (SR-IOV — see DOC-192, which blocks v2.1.0). Classification: 13 `supported-ui`, 7 `supported-derived`, 1 `supported-backend-only` (the hidden `miimon` hardcode), 3 `hidden-not-applicable`, 1 `docs-only-not-supported`, **39 `unknown-needs-review`** — the last group is the real output, since those families previously appeared in no ledger at all. **Four domains must never be conflated** and every citation must record which it proves: (A) Day-1 Agent `agent-config.yaml hosts[].networkConfig`, (B) Day-1 bare-metal IPI `install-config.yaml platform.baremetal.hosts[].networkConfig`, (C) post-install `NodeNetworkConfigurationPolicy`, (D) secondary-network Bond CNI (`xmitHashPolicy`, a different schema from NMState's `xmit_hash_policy`). **A and B are separate implementations in this codebase:** B supports only one ethernet interface with an IPv4 address, DNS and a default route — no bond, VLAN, IPv6, MTU, VRF or SR-IOV — so IPv6-only and dual-stack bare-metal IPI hosts cannot be expressed; recorded, not yet dispositioned. **Cross-minor state: fully aligned** — every catalog row, generator branch and UI control in this surface is identical at 4.20/4.21/4.22, consistent with the committed zero agent-config delta for 4.21.35→4.22.16. **Also found:** VRF is offered and emits structurally valid NMState but has **no Red Hat Day-1 documentation authority** (non-blocking because the output parses; needs a supportedness decision). **Recommended architecture: a dedicated version-aware `data/nmstate/<minor>/` authority** with scenario catalogs referencing it and a generated frontend mirror — Option 1 (synthesising nested paths into each scenario catalog) was rejected because it would require ~384 hand-maintained duplicate rows that must stay byte-identical, and `data/arch-support/<minor>.json` already proves the shared version-keyed-authority pattern in this repo. Each field carries `supportStatus`, min/max version, a same-minor citation **tagged with its domain**, mode/scenario applicability as data, validation metadata, and helper metadata. **Helper contract defined** (what/when/format/values[]/howUsed/dependencies[]/warnings[]/example plus conditionalHelp[], with `values[]` doubling as the validation enum so help and validation cannot drift). **Future-minor pipeline defined** — 8 fail-closed questions covering schema provenance, schema diff, Red Hat support-statement diff, field-level delta classification, continued validity of OAA's subset, frontend/backend mirror agreement, citation/help currency, and generated-output validation; **never inherit the previous minor's schema by default**. **Validator strategy:** pinned committed JSON schema validated in JS on every CI run (the control whose absence let DOC-192 ship), plus pinned-container `nmstatectl` at release certification; **no runtime `nmstatectl` dependency**, which would break the disconnected local-first model. **Release targets — v2.1.0: nothing from this item except DOC-192.** v2.1.x: the `data/nmstate/` authority, the generated-NMState schema guard, `miimon` made explicit, `balance-xor`, and the Red Hat-documented `options.primary`. Later: remaining bond options behind mode gating, `ipv6.autoconf`, `vlan.protocol`, domain-B parity, and the onboarding automation. **Children:** DOC-188 (advanced bond options), DOC-192 (the SR-IOV blocker). **Reconciles with** DOC-165 (broader generated-config coverage; this is its NMState slice) and PHX-007/PHX-025/PHX-034. |
| DOC-192 | **RELEASE BLOCKER** — SR-IOV generates an invalid NMState property that invalidates the whole host networkConfig | done_pending_verification | p0 | v2.1 Tranche 7A.1 NMState coverage audit (2026-10-08) | `backend/src/generate.js` `addEthernet()` (`if (sriov) entry.sriov = sriov;`); `backend/test/nic-bond-vlan-ipv6.test.js:571`; `frontend/src/components/NodeDrawerAgentContent.jsx` (SR-IOV checkbox + Total VFs input); `frontend/src/steps/HostInventoryStep.jsx` | **BLOCKS v2.1.0.** OAA emits SR-IOV as a **top-level `interfaces[].sriov: { total-vfs: N }`**. Both authorities define it as **`interfaces[].ethernet.sr-iov.total-vfs`** — the upstream NMState YAML API and Red Hat's own OCP 4.22 *Agent-based Installer* book §1.9, which documents `interfaces[].ethernet.sr-iov.total-vfs: 2` by example. No authority defines a top-level `sriov` interface key. **Consequence is not a silent drop:** NMState's deserializer is strict (serde `deny_unknown_fields`-style) and an unknown property aborts the parse — OpenShift's assisted-service surfaces it as "Invalid YAML string: unknown field" — so the **entire host `networkConfig` is rejected** and the Agent-based install fails at validation. **Fully reachable:** a visible "SR-IOV" checkbox and "SR-IOV Total VFs" input in the node drawer, on primary and additional interfaces, affecting every path that calls `addEthernet` (ethernet, bond members, vlan-on-ethernet, vlan-on-bond). Domain A (Agent) only; the bare-metal IPI builder never emits SR-IOV. All three minors. **Why it survived:** `nic-bond-vlan-ipv6.test.js:571` asserts `ethIface.sriov["total-vfs"] === 8` — **the test pins the defect as expected behaviour** — and nothing anywhere validates generated NMState against a schema. **Pre-existing** (present since the initial public release and shipped in v2.0.0); attribution does not make it acceptable, and the question asked is whether current v2.1.0 behaviour blocks. **Bounded corrective tranche 7A.2:** (1) emit `entry.ethernet = { ...entry.ethernet, "sr-iov": { "total-vfs": N } }`; (2) correct the test and add an assertion that no top-level `sriov` key exists on any interface; (3) add a structural guard asserting every emitted interface key is in a schema-derived allow-list, so this class of defect fails in CI rather than at install time; (4) cover 4.20/4.21/4.22; (5) record a product decision on whether SR-IOV remains offered at all — the Tranche 1 research already flags "whether Architect models SR-IOV at all is a product-scope question", and **either fixing the shape or removing the control is acceptable; shipping the current invalid output is not**. Explicitly out of scope for 7A.2: bond options, `balance-xor`, the `data/nmstate/` architecture, and every `unknown-needs-review` family. **Parent:** DOC-191. **CORRECTED IN TRANCHE 7B (2026-10-08) — no longer blocks v2.1.0.** Authority re-verified per minor before editing: the "Bonds and SR-IOV dual-NIC node network configuration" example at **§1.9 of the Agent-based Installer book in 4.20, 4.21 AND 4.22** all nest `ethernet:` → `sr-iov:` → `total-vfs:` (values 2, 2, 8; 4.21 additionally shows `vfs[]`), matching the upstream NMState YAML API. Post-install SR-IOV Operator config and Bond CNI were explicitly NOT used as evidence. **Blast radius confirmed:** Agent-Based Installer on Bare Metal or vSphere only — `buildAgentConfig` is the sole caller of `buildNmState` — affecting every `addEthernet` path (plain ethernet, bond members, vlan-on-ethernet, vlan-on-bond; primary and additional interfaces), equally at all three minors, including persisted/imported states. **Bare-metal IPI was never affected**: its separate inline builder reads a different state shape and has no SR-IOV path. **Fix:** one assignment in `addEthernet()` — `entry.ethernet = { ...entry.ethernet, "sr-iov": sriov }`, merging so a future ethernet-level field cannot be clobbered. The internal state property was deliberately NOT renamed: the defect was at the serialization seam, and renaming would force a migration for no functional gain. **Tests:** the assertion that pinned the bug now checks `ethIface.ethernet["sr-iov"]["total-vfs"]` **and** that no top-level `sriov` key exists; new `backend/test/t7b-sriov-nmstate-shape.test.js` adds **50** assertions covering the corrected nesting at 4.20/4.21/4.22 × bare-metal-agent and vsphere-agent, bond/VLAN paths, cross-minor shape identity, disabled/empty/non-numeric VF counts emitting nothing, byte-identical disabled output, and persisted-state regeneration. **The missing control is now in place:** a structural guard asserts every generated NMState key is in a schema-derived allow-list and is proven non-vacuous by rejecting an injected unknown key — so an invalid property now fails in CI rather than at install time. **No `nmstatectl` validation is claimed** (the local binary is a broken stub; nothing was installed). Status `done_pending_verification` pending the human's git commit. **Parent:** DOC-191. |

| PROD-030 | Container image and external binary digest pinning and verification | active | p1 | 2026-08-03 enterprise productization and security-ready distribution intake; re-confirmed as release-hardening blocker 2026-10-01 | `backend/Containerfile` (pinned OCP 4.21.36, per-arch SHA256, custom URL requires SHA256), `backend/src/ocMirrorRuntime.js` (runtime SHA256 verification, OC_MIRROR_URL requires OC_MIRROR_SHA256), `backend/test/binary-integrity.test.js` (8 tests) | **v2.0 critical sub-scope completed/pending commit:** (1) oc pinned to 4.21.36 with per-arch SHA256 verification at build time, (2) oc-mirror pinned to 4.21.36 with per-arch SHA256 verification at build time, (3) custom URL overrides (OCP_CLIENT_URL, OCP_MIRROR_URL) require corresponding SHA256 build-args — build fails without them, (4) runtime download verification: `ocMirrorRuntime.js` verifies SHA256 for mirror downloads and requires OC_MIRROR_SHA256 for custom OC_MIRROR_URL, (5) unknown architectures fail-hard, (6) `latest` URL eliminated (was resolving to oc 4.22.16). **POLICY CORRECTION (2026-10-02) — the external-tool sub-scope is NOT a pinning problem.** It has three distinct contracts; do not classify A or B as unresolved reproducibility defects. **(A) oc-mirror — intentional dynamic dependency.** Policy: global latest official oc-mirror + SHA256 verification against the channel's own `sha256sum.txt` + resolved-version provenance. Vendor-directed: Red Hat says use the latest oc-mirror v2 regardless of mirrored target release, and `--v2 list operators` does not exist in 4.21.x, so operator discovery *requires* a current binary. A later rebuild resolving a newer oc-mirror is intentional, not a defect. **(B) oc — target-version-aware dynamic dependency.** Policy: latest patch within the selected supported target minor (`clients/ocp/latest-<minor>`) + SHA256 verification + resolved-version provenance. Never global-latest, never a frozen patch. **(C) Remaining broader production-readiness scope — still active:** UBI 9 base image digest pinning, docker-compose.yml image tag pinning (`:latest` → versioned), `openshift-install` pinning/policy, SBOM/provenance relationships (PROD-023), signing relationships (PROD-031). **Do not close the PROD-030 umbrella on the strength of A and B alone.** Implementation: `backend/src/ocMirrorRuntime.js` (channel resolution, `acquireVerifiedTool`, provenance recording), `backend/Containerfile` (channel-resolved bake, no hardcoded z-stream), `backend/test/binary-integrity.test.js`. |

(2) Browser → backend Route: HTTPS (edge TLS terminates at OpenShift router) → HTTP to backend Service:4000 → backend pod. (3) Frontend pod proxying: absent — no Vite proxy, no nginx proxy, no reverse-proxy configuration exists; the frontend pod serves static assets only (currently via Vite dev server per PROD-026); the browser JavaScript in the user's browser makes API calls directly to the backend Route URL. There is no frontend-pod-to-backend-pod traffic path. **Frontend Route edge TLS: implemented** (route-frontend.yaml, edge termination with Redirect). **Backend Route edge TLS: implemented** (route-backend.yaml, edge termination with Redirect). **Browser-to-router encryption: implemented** (edge termination ensures HTTPS from browser to OpenShift router). **Router-to-Service/pod transport: unresolved** — edge termination means the OpenShift router terminates TLS and forwards plaintext HTTP to the backend Service on the cluster network; this leg is unencrypted. **Direct backend Route exposure: depends on PROD-038** — the backend Route is externally accessible; whether this should remain or be restricted to cluster-internal access depends on trust-model decision. **Required decisions (do not prescribe before trust model and topology are resolved):** retain edge termination with accepted internal-network risk and NetworkPolicy as compensating control; use re-encrypt termination (router re-encrypts to backend using service-serving certificate); use backend application TLS; use service-serving certificates (OpenShift service-ca); use a service mesh for mTLS; remove direct backend Route exposure entirely; document compensating NetworkPolicy controls (PROD-027). |

Backend uses MOCK_MODE, FEEDBACK_MODE (in `backend/src/feedback.js`), APP_REPO from docker-compose.yml. | **Secret classification, wiring, and external secret-manager contract.** Required in this order: (1) Classify each secret and credential (deployment-time infrastructure secret vs user-session-provided credential — pull secrets are currently provided per-session by the user, not injected at deploy time), (2) Decide deployment-time vs user-session ownership per credential based on PROD-038 trust-model decision, (3) Remove obsolete placeholder Secret fields or wire valid deployment-time secrets to the Deployment, (4) Define projected read-only Secret volume behavior for any wired secrets, (5) Define a vendor-neutral integration contract for external secret managers (External Secrets Operator, Vault, Sealed Secrets, or customer-native equivalent), (6) Verify rotation, redaction, export-bundle exclusion, and release-bundle exclusion behavior. **Dependencies:** PROD-038 (trust model determines which secrets are deployment-scoped vs user-session-scoped).  **Not closed by the Tranche Security Gate (DOC-171).** The gate proves credentials are not persisted to SQLite or browser storage; it does not provide Kubernetes Secret integration for credential supply. ## Production readiness phase sequencing

Production deployment should follow this phased approach to minimize risk.

**Source:** Original phases 1–3 from production readiness assessment 2026-04-24. Phase 4 (Enterprise Hardening) and Phase 5 (Enterprise Distribution) added from 2026-08-03 enterprise productization and security-ready distribution intake.

| phase | name | items | rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| Phase 1 (Critical) | Before first production deploy | PROD-001, PROD-002, PROD-003, PROD-004, PROD-005, PROD-006, PROD-007 | Critical correctness, observability, and operational requirements; block production deploy until complete. **Status:** PROD-001/002/004/005/006/007 verified_done; PROD-003 done_pending_verification (restricted-v2 claim unverified). Phase 1 completion does not establish production readiness — it addresses the original 2026-04-24 assessment scope only. Enterprise hardening (Phases 4–5) and trust-model decisions (PROD-038) are separate prerequisites for any network-exposed or customer-facing deployment. |
| Phase 2 (Important) | First 30 days in production | PROD-008, PROD-009, PROD-010, PROD-011, PROD-012, PROD-013, PROD-014 | Important operational maturity; address during initial production stabilization period. **Status: PROD-008/009/010 verified_done; PROD-011 done_pending_verification; PROD-012/013/014 active.** |
| Phase 3 (Enhancement) | First 90 days in production | PROD-015, PROD-016, PROD-017, PROD-018, PROD-019, PROD-020, PROD-021, PROD-022, PROD-023 | Operational excellence and hardening; enhance monitoring, security, documentation. |
| Phase 4 (Enterprise Hardening) | Before hosted/shared deployment | PROD-024, PROD-025, PROD-026, PROD-027, PROD-028, PROD-029, PROD-032, PROD-034, PROD-035, PROD-036, PROD-039 | Enterprise security and deployment hardening. Required before any network-exposed, hosted, or multi-user deployment. PROD-026 (p1) addresses demonstrated Vite dev-server exposure in production container. PROD-024/025 (p1) depend on PROD-038 trust-model decision. |
| Phase 5 (Enterprise Distribution) | Before customer delivery | PROD-030, PROD-031, PROD-033, PROD-037, PROD-038, PROD-040, PROD-041, PROD-042, PROD-043, PROD-044, PROD-045, PROD-046 | Secure distribution, assurance documentation, and lifecycle operations. Required for customer-facing or partner-distributed artifacts. PROD-038 (trust model) is the dependency root and should be resolved first. PROD-041 is the umbrella tracking item. |

## Execution sequencing (DOC-030 to DOC-041)

This sequence is intended to maximize risk reduction and unblock dependent work.

| order | item_id | rationale |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | DOC-031 | Full disconnected-scenario audit is foundational; prevents building features on incorrect scenario assumptions. |
| 2 | DOC-035 | `platform: none` support boundary depends on audit outcomes and OpenShift 4.20 doc-grounded scope. |
| 3 | DOC-039 | Methodology/sub-scenario decision intelligence depends on validated scenario matrix from DOC-031 and DOC-035. |
| 4 | DOC-033 | Root device hints correctness is a high-confidence correctness bug and should be fixed early. |
| 5 | DOC-036 | Import override issue is a user-facing data integrity bug; fix before larger UX expansions. |
| 6 | DOC-030 | Per-operation log download is operationally valuable and low-risk once operations data semantics are confirmed. |
| 7 | DOC-037 | High/low-side mode design defines runtime capability gating model used by several later enhancements. |
| 8 | DOC-038 | High-side hardening profile builds directly on DOC-037 operating-mode boundaries. |
| 9 | DOC-040 | UPI simplification should follow audit + methodology + mode/hardening baselines to avoid inconsistent helpers. |
| 10 | DOC-032 | Cross-scenario layout normalization is broad and should follow correctness and scenario-truth fixes. |
| 11 | DOC-034 | Persistent live YAML drawer depends on stable per-tab relevance and UX/layout conventions from DOC-032. |
| 12 | DOC-041 | Feedback mechanism should be implemented after core usability/correctness updates to capture meaningful usage feedback. |

## Dependency map (DOC-030 to DOC-041)

|---|---|---|
| DOC-035 | DOC-031 | disconnected scenario audit completed with explicit supported/unsupported matrix |
| DOC-039 | DOC-031, DOC-035 | scenario constraint model documented and accepted |
| DOC-038 | DOC-037 | high/low-side capability gating model defined |
| DOC-040 | DOC-031, DOC-039 | scenario/methodology constraints finalized for UPI helper scope |
| DOC-034 | DOC-032 | tab/section consistency baseline established |
| DOC-041 | DOC-037, DOC-038 | mode/hardening/privacy constraints defined for secure feedback design |

## Intake template for future recommendations

Use this template for new requests (for example, "support platform none scenarios across the app"):

```markdown
### request_id: REQ-XXX
- request_title:
- category: docs | helper | backlog | governance | feature
- scope: global | scenario-specific
- scenario_ids:
- motivation:
- affected_sources:
- initial_priority: p0 | p1 | p2 | p3
- status: active
- validation_requirements:
- owner:
```

## Migration rule for old status references

When updating any status-bearing working doc:

1. Keep historical narrative if useful.
2. Add: `Canonical status: docs/BACKLOG_STATUS.md`.
3. Remove references to untracked/local-only backlog files.

## Low-risk migration sequence

Use this order to reduce churn and avoid context loss:

1. Establish canonical authority links (`docs/INDEX.md`, this file, `docs/HELPER_USAGE.md`, `AI_GOVERNANCE.md`).
2. Normalize broken/missing references to tracked docs.
3. Reconcile status labels in high-traffic docs and add superseded pointers.
4. Reconcile machine-readable contradictions (scenario IDs, docs host links) with evidence.
5. Add archive banners to superseded historical docs after their key context is captured here.

## Conflict resolution workflow

1. If two docs disagree, open or update one item row in this file.
2. Attach evidence paths from code/tests/CI to the row.
3. Set canonical status and next action in this file.
4. In older docs, add a short note pointing to this item row as canonical status.
